# Run Supervision and Self-Contained Outcomes

Status: Accepted future supervision contract with separately identified implemented AX/evidence slices; full supervision is not released. Proposed supervision API/policy additions and their examples are not supported by the current release. [BACKLOG.md](../BACKLOG.md) owns the next selective AX/navigation/existing-runtime release and the remaining future work; this document owns behavior and evidence boundaries. The selective release does not activate independent owner-loss supervision, execution deadlines, public completion streams or unapproved source/retention expansion. Native authority, unchanged credentials, the agreed three-platform scope and positive closure requirements remain mandatory for the future feature. MPRIS and removal of `inspect` stay excluded.

## Goal and Boundaries

A coordinator should receive a finite Run's outcome without polling. Optional periodic diagnostics make overdue work discoverable, and an independent execution deadline can terminate an owned generation. Neither policy is required for ordinary jobs or controlled services.

- Preserve `spawn`, `message`, and `inspect`. Ordinary Run inspection is a manual request for the same compact snapshot used by timed diagnostics, with a readable context index for selective investigation. Retain resolution, activation, Control, and runtime diagnosis; do not remove existing expert routes implicitly.
- Do not add resumable suspension, checkpoints, automatic restarts/retries, a synchronous wait budget, global supervision defaults, or a new actor type.
- Do not treat a diagnostic heartbeat as progress, semantic attention, or evidence that a task is healthy.
- Preserve owner filtering, process-identity proof, lifecycle locks, parent/root delivery lineage, redaction, retention boundaries, and native Pi history.
- The current [Run lifecycle](./async-runs.md), [Recipe standard](./template-recipes.md), [inspection contract](./inspection.md), and [coordinator delivery](./coordinator-delivery.md) remain the shipped behavior until implementation lands.

## First-Layer UX/AX: Progress, Decision and Drill-Down

The accepted direction applies at every public level: give the human an understandable process state, give the agent enough compressed context for its next decision, and offer only a relevant way to inspect deeper evidence. Compact output is selected meaning, not a truncated raw dump. Full structured detail remains explicit; a pointer is data, not body, work or native authority. Do not promise a later completion unless its delivery is actually established.

Locally implemented Control receipts now follow AX in the existing `tools-message.ts` owner: flat Markdown-list records identify run/action without a duplicated namespace and keep result, delivery, handling and observed Run state distinct. Queued and delivered both leave handling unconfirmed; archive/prune reports its completed operation, while legacy kill reports attempt plus unconfirmed termination. Multiline values retain original line separators/internal Markdown under an outer indentation. At most one contextual next node names the inspect tool and its target/view: actor-delivery evidence uses Control, a stop attempt uses Trace, and changed owner/generation directs the caller to its own Run inventory instead of a blind retry. Unrecognized custom advisory text stays an indented instruction leaf, never guessed arguments or automatic execution. Already-terminal refusal needs no routine extra inspection. Public input schemas and underlying action/status facts remain unchanged; advisory next_actions now agree with the compact hint. Full structured detail remains explicit via verbose JSON.

The kill record says **result: kill attempted** and **termination: unconfirmed**. This is a presentation correction, not a new stop claim or native proof: legacy `killed/stopped` fields, immediate terminal progress/handled markers, caller-generation preconditions and B2/C integration remain unresolved. Trace records the attempted action, not whole-scope closure. A failed tool call after actuation may also have partial effects; compact returned-result improvements do not resolve that exception path or justify automatic retry. Public kill/archive/prune refusals for changed owner or generation now omit the replacement's nested status from compact/details/verbose output, while retaining the original action/refusal facts and owned-inventory hint. The omission is a public response restriction, not a new observation or permission; same-identity already-terminal detail remains available, and privileged kernel results are unchanged. Controlled source/packed witnesses cover all three actions and both replacement reasons without signals or workload. Explicit verbosity is not extra ownership authority.

Runtime error presentation now fences its argument-repair heuristic at dispatch: caller value normalization and synchronous missing-value errors before the first executor entry retain their existing usage guide; after any executor entry or async-launcher handoff, the public adapter rethrows the original value without rebuilding an Error from its text. AggregateError members, cause and details remain original. A later template step can fail after earlier controlled effects, so missing-value wording is not evidence that the invocation was unapplied. Async launcher-side validation remains its original message rather than being guessed to be caller-repair guidance. The local dispatch bit is not an admission/actuation/closure receipt; pre-dispatch guidance likewise proves no absence of preparation effects. Existing retry policies, result formatting, error spacing and source/native permissions are unchanged. Source and packed controlled ports cover plain/delegated execution, early/late missing inputs and async handoff-option faults without new native workloads; these are not positive native partial-actuation or SDK rendered-phone acceptance.

Launch first-layer inspection confirmed that real new-generation metadata already reports running; no extra liveness or fabricated accepted-state reader is needed. Compact launch receipts now expose the existing reused flag as **existing Run reused**, including the bounded core fallback. A complete actually shown direct-location row replaces the redundant session-wide map hint; absent, unavailable or wholly omitted rows retain that optional request. All exact selected location values/roles, JSON-compatible expert details and the shared 16 KiB compact bound remain. The older shared handoff function and completion formatter retain their text, so saved batch versions/replay are unchanged; new AX launch receipts now use the separately rendered typed request. Two existing pure launch contract groups live with tools-response and remain selected by conformance. The compact launch slice now renders bare block YAML, opaque scalar contents, native model/thinking groups and one typed optional next node. Its whole nested navigation row and16KiB exact-fit/one-byte-over refusal are covered by the response owner, including reuse and optional-field fallback; missing navigation cannot evade the bound. Prior full source/npm SDK observations in this section describe the earlier compact syntax. Updated launch-only fixture parsing retains those assertions and old saved-message support, but fresh full SDK/native acceptance remains separate. Both public spawn and async registered tools use this existing owner; dispatch errors/verbose schemas are unchanged. Control's unmigrated field and inspect-action primitives keep their byte-exact receipts; compact launch uses the shared YAML emitter instead. The whole tools-response owner is replayed from the actual installed dist. SDK fixture grammar assertions change only for the compact launch text; identity, lifecycle, source, native absence, cleanup and completion-codec properties remain. No packet version, clock/controller default or new source authority was introduced. The retained source/packed SDK journeys still require actual launch/terminal reference reads without extra inspection or new workload permutations; headless formatter checks are not a fresh execution of those journeys.

Source properties exercise real public already-terminal refusal, archive/prune and identity-change routes with isolated controlled metadata, plus data-only phase/opacity/hint witnesses. The packed compiled owner is checked without a real kill. These checks do not prove rendered TUI/phone behavior, actual native closure or a later terminal delivery. The canonical backlog retains first-layer outcome/failure and owner-specific partial-failure work plus the gated stop integration; this section is not a second task list.

### Cross-Surface Presentation Contract

The project-wide [Agent Experience (AX) standard](./agent-experience.md) is the canonical presentation contract for Inspector, public tool text/errors and agent-bound context. It combines Markdown readability with named structural context, defaults to flat list/key-value records, nests only for meaningful relationships and preserves embedded Markdown as one value. Its syntax, omission markers, examples and acceptance rules live there, not in a second release-specific style manual.

Status: Adopted design, rollout incomplete. The next selective release targets independently ready AX behavior through the existing surface owners, subject to its own source/packed and loaded acceptance. It neither waits for excluded future features nor waives applicable source/privacy/generation/clock/native gates. Current one-line receipts, brace-based detail rendering and older saved formatting described below remain implementation evidence, not the new target. New capture presentation must keep old packet bytes stable; neither the standard nor a renderer acquires source/body permission, result freshness or lifecycle authority. [BACKLOG.md](../BACKLOG.md) owns implementation slices and live acceptance.

### Inspector Chronology: Bounded Implemented Subset

The human Inspector now consumes shared newer-higher recorded-observation ranks before source/display clipping, rather than filtered row positions. Normal retained canonical101→102 keeps100 visible rows with advancing numbers; source filtering and scrolling preserve the shared basis. Projection keys/timestamps/ordinals come from existing bounded reads and pre-tail session metadata, without wider bodies, a new clock or persistent numbering service. Missing/lost/private/ambiguous metadata, known bounded-history loss, metadata overflow and inconsistent legacy equal-time joins show `#?` with a visible reason; no exact original lifetime offset is invented. Mutable snapshots/late evidence can rerank a later observation but do not change stable event identity or mint a canonical event. Existing ordering, source counters/detail, body budgets, selection and saved packets remain independent.

[Actor Inspector](./actor-inspector.md#chronological-numbering-requested-replacement) owns the detailed human contract. Source and actual npm compiled owner properties cover the bounded behavior, width/scroll/filter/selection and rejection boundaries; synthetic on-disk fixtures are not authenticated producer history. Live colored-terminal/phone acceptance and operator-authorized reload remain gated, as do integrated release/native/clock authority. This human-only display change adds no agent tool parameter or public Trace JSON field and does not belong in the main actors operating Skill.

### User-Approved Omission Disclosure

Agent-facing information/outcome messages may contain selected body previews as well as file references; they are not location-only envelopes. The latest approved display target is **[omitted: N chars]** or **[omitted: unknown]**, with U+2026 at actual display-gap boundaries and the shared cross-surface structure above. Exact N is an admitted Unicode code-point count for the already-authorized privacy-projected source. This target is not yet implemented uniformly; each owned projection below distinguishes its implemented spelling from immutable older formats. ### Control Partial-Failure Evidence: Existing Facts, Not a New Receipt Contract

The three mutation paths have different evidence boundaries; one generic exception-to-status conversion cannot truthfully recover them:

- **Legacy stop — source inspection, not a new signal experiment.** In async-runs, stopRun validates ownership/generation/process identity, calls signalOwnedRunProcess, then separately appends Trace, marks terminal handling, finalizes interrupted execution and writes progress. A signal/helper attempt can precede every one of those persistence steps. A missing Trace/handled/progress record therefore proves no absence of actuation; a present legacy record proves no whole-scope closure. Native helper failure may also follow an attempted group/tree operation or fallback. The returned signal plan is not retained as an authoritative partial-stop receipt on every exception path. Do not manufacture stopped:false, scope_closed, a signal count or an unapplied claim from a thrown error. B2/C and the caller-generation boundary still own the missing stop protocol.
- **Actor-local Control — controlled journal counterexample plus source inspection.** runs-control-delivery admits a queued record before endpoint resolution/transport, then records delivered separately. A controlled namedPipeSend callback can mark that exact record claimed/handled and subsequently throw; the caller receives delivery_failed with the original admitted control_id/generation while the journal correctly remains handled. This witness proves monotonic recorded handling, not actual native execution or Windows support. A delivery exception is not a handling failure or permission to replay. The delivery owner now retains its surfaced primary value in non-enumerable Error.cause and, when failed-status recording also throws, ordered AggregateError.errors with the original metadata/message for that branch. Endpoint-not-ready creates its existing logical failure before persistence so a later write failure cannot erase its genuinely admitted id/generation. A post-send delivered-record fault is a persistence failure, not evidence that transport did nothing; subsequent failure-record faults retain both. Failure-text coercion may itself throw: preserve that value after the original cause and use bounded vocabulary failure text unavailable, never replay a hostile getter/stringification. Ordinary single-failure messages/reasons stay compatible; newly combined FIFO write/close failures have a distinct static summary while preserving both exact causes and already-written bytes. Raw cause/errors remain Kernel-local and non-enumerable under ordinary JSON/object spread, not a new public schema or complete host-format privacy certificate. Source and actual npm tests reuse controlled transport/write faults and the existing real FIFO fixture; the FIFO fault writes one byte, actually closes its fd, then throws a controlled close error, proving retained partial bytes without another workload or closure claim. The sendRunControl facade now preserves its surfaced admission/awaited-delivery rejection if lifecycle release also throws: standard ordered non-enumerable AggregateError errors/cause retains exact original then release values, including undefined/primitive/original AggregateError, without field access, coercion or flattening. A single rejection and release-only failure after successful transport remain exact. Controlled live-host port fixtures actually admit one Control, record claimed→handled, keep the lifecycle lock held while transport is pending, then reject/resolve transport before the injected release fault. A contender proves the same existing lock interval, not kill independence; no runner/socket is launched or signalled. Every pending call settles before builtin restoration, fixture identity is captured before await and state/host_exit:not_asserted remain retained. Getter-based admission failures cover no-send/no-record paths without inventing an id. Existing conservative public errors still withhold saved correlation and keep private nested causes. This cause-only change leaves return await/endpoint-generation locking untouched and does not implement B2. Failures before a Control is returned and opaque lower-journal/file-state masking remain separate: do not reconstruct a missing id, infer exhaustive successful release/native closure or recover a cause already lost below the facade. The actor-local public adapter now catches rejections only around sendRunControl, after existing request/access/declaration checks, and throws a new bounded Error retaining the exact rejection as non-enumerable cause. It never coerces the raw value or copies error fields: message text contains only a flat AX record of caller-owned run/action, delivery/handling uncertainty, a multiline possible-effects/no-replay warning and one structured fresh-owned-inventory next action. A whole oversized id yields run_omission with reason/shown/omitted/total; no saved correlation or raw exception field is read to build the public copy. Verbose does not widen it. Admission correlation remains Kernel-local even for an unchanged logical session: retained ids do not prove active reply scope after await or A→B→A. Target text is whole or explicitly omitted under the existing 16 KiB model bound, reserving the complete safety summary. Actual Pi 1.0 public runToolCall verifies error/isError normalization against source and npm code; a controlled pending named-pipe port with the live test host's observed identity preserves handled journal state across context/owner/generation changes, not native execution, Windows support or host exit. The fixture launches no runner/socket, retains live-host state, and settles every pending call before restoring builtins. Recipient-bound correlation enrichment now remains host-contract/adoption-gated after completed preparation, not permission to copy raw fields. Do not synthesize a missing id or expose the raw input/endpoint/journal as compact context. Existing owned Control inspection is the selective route when correlation is available; it is not a fresh transport/handling guarantee.
- **Retirement — source/npm controlled filesystem counterexamples.** runs-retention still records queued before archive rename/tombstone creation or artifact-copy/prune removal, then handled. Manual retirement now preserves a single surfaced rejection exactly, including primitive/undefined and original AggregateError, and keeps later formatter/failed-record/lifecycle-release faults in ordered private AggregateError errors with cause fixed to that first rejection. Formatter text is attempted once; hostile conversion adds its exact failure and uses failure text unavailable. The sibling journal appender separately preserves append plus release failures as a nested boundary, never flattening original aggregates. Actual controlled rename/copy/removal witnesses retain already changed bytes with one action, including completed removal before a later handled-record error. A committed queued record can fail its release before evidence id is returned and before operation admission: do not invent that missing return/correlation. Handled bytes can coexist with a later failed record or lifecycle-release failure; no record is rewritten into rollback, atomic receipt or native closure. Faulted lock-scope fixtures retain state and assert no native workload/host exit. Standard guards, ordinary success/refusal fields, TTL, raw-cause privacy and source selection remain separate; existing lower-layer already-masked exceptions cannot be recovered here. A Run-local Trace may no longer exist after prune, and no new public failure DTO or permission to follow arbitrary archive/artifact bodies is added. Do not repeat archive/prune merely to obtain verbosity.
- **Lifecycle/release — source inspection.** sendRunControl currently retains its lifecycle lock across await deliverRunControl. This legacy path does not satisfy the accepted no-awaited-transport-under-lifecycle-admission rule and cannot certify kill independence from a stalled sender. Blindly removing await moves release ahead of transport completion and changes the endpoint/generation race: equivalent current-generation admission/transport fencing must be resolved before such a change. Finally-based release remains another effect boundary. The generic synchronous withFileMutationLock wrapper now preserves an exact surfaced callback rejection and a later release fault in ordered non-enumerable standard AggregateError errors/cause, without field access, coercion, flattening or re-execution. A single callback/release-only failure or normal result remains exact, including undefined; returned thenables are not inspected and no async semantics is added. Actual controlled owner/contender/file-write witnesses retain the same held token/interval and written bytes across injected Error/undefined removal-boundary faults. Faulted owner/frame/lock state remains retained; this is not a new native workload, host exit, rollback, exhaustive release proof or public receipt. Existing acquisition/reclaim/atomic writer behavior stays separate, as do direct acquire/release callers and any lower fault already masked before the wrapper. A release call is still not positive cleanup proof.
- **Atomic replacement — source/npm controlled writer counterexamples.** The shared file-state replacement loop now keeps a single surfaced rename rejection exactly, including null/undefined/primitives and original aggregates. Null-safe guarded retry-code observation retains an accessor rejection after the original in private non-enumerable standard AggregateError errors/cause; neither value is coerced or flattened. Unavailable/nonmatching/failed classification does not retry. Ordinary EPERM/EBUSY retry behavior remains 20 attempts / 19 actual 50ms waits, and onBeforeReplace stays once. Real temporary text/JSON writer fixtures inject rejection before or after actual rename, verify old/new bytes independently of the thrown value and preserve post-commit effects without replay/rollback. Getter failures include Error/undefined; hostile text/code values are not coerced. Actual retry witnesses preserve eventual success, exact exhausted last error and existing waits. Builtins restore after synchronous settle; fault frames remain and no new native workload, native/host exit, fsync/power-loss, source/body or transaction authority is asserted. Unsurfaced best-effort staging cleanup and any lower exception already discarded remain separate.

No new public failure DTO, native receipt, source permission, default status switch or lifecycle implementation is adopted by this reconciliation. First preserve the distinct owners' causal/correlation evidence, then derive a compact explanation only from facts actually available to the original recipient. Unavailable facts stay unknown; no automatic retry or retrospective receipt reconstruction. BACKLOG owns remaining recipient-bound correlation, legacy summary/omission permission and gated stop/transport-admission work; completed owner-specific delivery/retirement/facade/mutation/replacement cause preservation does not close them.

#### Recipient-Bound Correlation Preparation

Preparation is complete for optional actor-local failure correlation on the inspected Pi1.0.0 public SDK boundary. The current conservative error remains the implemented contract; no new field, lifetime registry, public status switch, host-floor increase or transport-lock change is adopted.

- **Kernel admission versus public reply:** tools-access checks the invoking logical session against Run ownership before dispatch; admitted error ids/generation are private historical facts. Neither a matching logical id, retained manager, copied error nor verbose request proves the recipient of a later error result. Missing returned admission correlation is never reconstructed.
- **Existing actors wrapper:** extension-runtime's withCurrentThinkingContext currently spreads the SDK context into a plain object before invoking core tools. This snapshots manager/signal data and does not retain the SDK's lazy getter revocation as a reply capability. The private opt-in supervision epoch pins manager/clock-owner lifetime for its own source admission, not Control result publication; borrowing it or adding another registry is not an approved remedy.
- **Public SDK guards:** ExtensionRunner creates fresh context wrappers whose getters call runner.assertActive. Invalidation on disposal/reload rejects later guarded access, but a completed tool's context remains readable while that runner lives, and a copied manager reference remains ordinary readable data after invalidation. Context signal is the current operation getter and may be undefined for idle public tool execution; cancellation is not ownership or atomic result publication.
- **Result pipeline:** public agent-core runToolCall catches a thrown Error into text/isError, then may await afterToolCall before returning. It has no extension-context/recipient check at that final boundary. The owning property `SDK context guards do not grant an atomic recipient lease for Control error enrichment` uses actual registered SDK-wrapped tool contexts and public runToolCall, not fake context getters: two calls mint different wrappers with the same manager; the first remains readable after completion; a pending post-tool hook permits controlled SDK disposal after normalization; guarded reads then reject, a spread copy still reads the old manager and the authored candidate marker still returns as an error. No real correlation or Actor action is published by the probe.

This is a bounded public-SDK/core-helper counterexample to treating getter validity or cached equality as an atomic reply lease, not proof of a live AgentSessionRuntime session-switch leak. The normal runtime awaits abort before shutdown/invalidation; public runToolCall itself emits no transcript/events, and the probe makes no provider/model/network request, starts no Actor/runner/socket and asserts no native/host exit. Existing actual public Control source/npm properties separately retain private causes and handled facts across stable/session/A→B→A/manager/owner/generation changes.

The exact remaining gate is a reviewed trusted host/recipient-publication contract covering the original tool call, error normalization, result hooks and final routing, together with fresh owner/generation checks and explicit permission for selective admitted fields. A host integration might supply that contract; this assessment neither mandates an upstream SDK change nor silently adopts one. Until it is approved and proved on supported source/npm host journeys, keep withholding saved correlation and use fresh owned inventory. The optional enrichment does not block the already-delivered conservative error path and must not reopen completed cause preservation or B2 locking. Do not repeat this preparation without changed API/contract evidence.

## Exact Launch API

Add two independent optional fields to the root JSON/Markdown Recipe contract and the public `spawn` request:

- `report_every: Duration | null`: Optional minimum interval between eligible diagnostic deliveries for this generation; at least `1m` (`60000ms`) when enabled. It is disabled by default.
- `max_runtime: Duration | null`: Maximum admitted execution duration for this generation, followed by runtime-owned termination.

`Duration` is a string matching `^[1-9][0-9]*(ms|s|m|h)$`. Normalize to positive integer milliseconds; reject overflow or an unrepresentable deadline before launching work. Numbers, zero, negatives, fractions, whitespace, compound durations, booleans, and empty strings are invalid. `null` is the only explicit disable value; omission means inherit, not disable.

`report_every` has an exact lower bound of `1m`: `60000ms`, `60s`, and `1m` are equivalent, while `59999ms` and `59s` are rejected, not rounded or clamped. This is a minimum, not quantization into whole-minute steps: `60001ms` is valid. `max_runtime` is independent and keeps the positive-duration grammar without this floor, so `500ms`, `30s`, or even `1ms` can be execution ceilings. Neither setting implicitly enables the other.

Planned Recipe example:

```json
{
  "async": true,
  "report_every": "2m",
  "max_runtime": "20m",
  "template": "generate-report"
}
```

Planned launch override:

```json
{
  "recipe": "project-work/repo-health",
  "report_every": "1m",
  "max_runtime": null
}
```

Resolve each field independently: explicit launch value, including `null`, overrides the effective root Recipe; an omitted Recipe field inherits through direct delegation; an unresolved field is disabled. A derived root Recipe's explicit value or `null` overrides the delegated source. There is no implicit interval or deadline.

These are literal Run policy fields, not template flags or inferred placeholders. Do not interpret `values.report_every`, `values.max_runtime`, or similarly named actor arguments as supervision. Do not add reserved arguments to every registered tool. Async registered tools inherit their effective root Recipe policies; per-launch overrides in this increment are exposed through `spawn`. An inline synchronous execution path must reject root supervision fields as `run_supervision_requires_run`, not silently ignore an apparent execution limit. `spawn` always creates a Run and can therefore honor the fields even when the source Recipe is not marked async.

Named imports and command graph nodes are not Runs: their Recipe-level policies do not establish or reset the enclosing Run's timers. Separately spawned children have independent policies, while an expired parent's owned-tree teardown still applies. Direct Recipe/tool specialization must retain the resolved policy through the existing contract resolver rather than copying its template or rediscovering a different source.

All four combinations are supported: diagnostics only, deadline only, both, or neither. Absence of both does not make a finite task infinite; it disables these two automatic policies only. Existing terminal delivery and shutdown/parent teardown remain in force.

### Effective Policy and Clock Identity

Persist the effective millisecond values, their Recipe/launch/unset provenance, and one generation start anchor before workload execution. Return the following normalized fields alongside the existing launch metadata; `run` remains the logical id without the `run:` target prefix:

```json
{
  "run": "review",
  "run_instance_id": "<opaque generation>",
  "started_at": "<ISO timestamp>",
  "supervision": {
    "report_every_ms": 120000,
    "max_runtime_ms": 1200000,
    "deadline_at": "<ISO timestamp>"
  }
}
```

Disabled millisecond fields are `null`; `deadline_at` is `null` when `max_runtime_ms` is disabled. The initial launch response also exposes the authorized `cwd` and `evidence` pointers defined below, so diagnosis does not depend on receiving a later heartbeat.

Elapsed wall duration starts once at generation admission and includes startup, retries, delays, and nested work; it is not CPU time or time since the last report. Use a monotonic clock while the supervisor remains alive and persist the absolute deadline for reattachment. Recovery must not calculate a fresh `now + max_runtime`, replay missed intervals, or silently reset an unprovable clock. A missed deadline is acted on when trustworthy supervision resumes; clock uncertainty is diagnostic evidence, not permission to fabricate a new budget.

Private [`run-supervision.ts`](../lib/run-supervision.ts) owns stored policy/clocks, runtime-local timing and the deadline wake protocol together; its timing primitives do not establish independent supervision. `captureRunSupervisionTimeOrigin` takes the wall sample once at generation admission and retains its monotonic start/provider/epoch in a WeakMap behind the exact frozen origin reference. `createRunSupervisionTimebase` decodes the existing stored-clock contract, requires that genuine origin and matching captured start, binds one immutable policy and rejects copied/serialized origins, changed policies and wholly absent/invalid clocks without a current-time fallback. Same-runtime late handles share the original elapsed and continuity state, not renewed allowance; owner/generation admission is still a separate obligation. `observe()` reads only monotonic samples, snapshots each field once before validation/arithmetic, floors display elapsed and ceils positive remaining/wake milliseconds so sub-millisecond evidence cannot fire early. Subsequent wall-clock jumps do not retime it. Deadlines over 2,147,483,647 ms yield bounded wake slices recomputed from the original origin, never clamped policy duration or missed-tick replay. Disabled deadlines yield no deadline wake; report cadence/acceptance remains separate. A throwing, reversed, nonfinite/unsafe or changed-epoch source latches first uncertainty across every handle for that origin, stops further source reads and returns unknown elapsed/remaining. Previously proven due facts remain due, not pending, but grant no actuation or scope authority. Frozen stored policy and absolute deadline remain unchanged. Coordinator/independent-runtime origin ownership, cross-runtime controller authority, independent live scheduling, trusted restart recovery after origin loss, terminal timing integration and public/source/packed controller journeys remain open; controlled-source tests and wake plans do not prove native enforcement or real controller independence.

Private [`createRunSupervisionController`](../lib/run-supervision.ts) now owns one deadline wake lifecycle over that timebase. It consumes an exact frozen Run/generation/optional-owner binding and synchronous caller-admitted identity/clock reads; it does not itself admit canonical storage or reconstruct authority from metadata. Before observation and again before arming/notifying, it requires the same identity and normalized immutable stored clock. Disabled ceilings (including reporting-only policy) create no deadline timer or identity polls. Enabled controllers use bounded host `setTimeout` by default or a controlled asynchronous scheduler, recomputing remaining time from the original origin on early/long/overdue wakes without fixed-tick replay or renewed allowance. Safe-integer remaining time uses integer elapsed subtraction so a fractional sample cannot lose a millisecond to large-number cancellation. `start()` is idempotent and cannot restart a stopped/unproven/due controller; `snapshot()` never samples state or clock. Ticket revocation fences duplicate, stale and post-stop callbacks before cancellation or handoff. Invalid/throwing/inline timer providers, binding/clock changes, invalid timing, clock uncertainty and due-observer failure retain bounded failure without implicit retry; missing/failed cancellation stays explicitly `timer_cleanup: "unproven"`, not proof that a physical host timer was removed. Reentrant stop from a port cannot rearm or resurrect the watcher. A previously proven due fact can survive later clock uncertainty. At most one immutable `run_deadline_due` observation is handed off per controller; it says `stop_requested: false` and `scope_closure: "not_asserted"`, is not durable delivery acceptance, and never signals, appends a stop claim, finalizes status, mutates files or touches routine report cadence. Ports are kernel-owned synchronous contracts, not awaited model/work hooks or actor policy fields. Coordinator/independent-runtime composition, cross-runtime controller authority, trusted restart recovery, durable B2 arbitration and terminal timing still need integration. A controlled-provider matrix and actual Node-timer smoke exercise this protocol, but do not prove an independent supervisor process, hung-host behavior, native closure, public strict-deadline support or installed/public controller journeys; C and the combined release gate remain unchanged.

Private [`runs-supervision.ts`](../lib/runs-supervision.ts) now supplies canonical state ports rather than relying on caller-created identity/clock DTOs. `readRunSupervisionBinding(stateDir, expected)` freshly admits exact published Run/generation/optional owner through the shared context-identity owner, then independently reads and decodes captured clock metadata under its 1 MiB source bound; the marker retains its separate 16 KiB bound. Only frozen identity/clock and an observation-change callback are returned, never values, private tokens, local paths, lifecycle/result facts or role bodies. Missing/partial/coercible/contradictory clocks remain unavailable without a current-time read, actor-value fallback or metadata repair. `createRunSupervisionControllerFromState` requires the original genuine runtime origin and assembles but does not automatically start/register the deadline controller. Its real `readCurrent` port rereads canonical metadata at each check, preserving root-directory/marker object identity across the controller lifetime. Fresh same-generation PID/status/other non-clock updates are allowed; policy/start/deadline changes, replaced same-generation state/markers and stale owners/generations fence callbacks, never retarget to latest state. A per-read metadata stamp protects the observation window, not a promise to freeze all metadata for the whole lifetime. No result/progress/stop claim/export/ACK/clock file is written, no process probed and no work/producer/native authority reconstructed. Construction may bind retained origin policy before a later source-change refusal; it does not roll that binding back or capture a substitute origin. The strict capped JSON path now requires the complete declared file: premature EOF after an otherwise valid JSON prefix is unavailable, not admission of an unread malformed trailer. The shared identity/index descriptor reader has the same full-read guard, and bounded ownership-marker reads select strict UTF-8/full reading; default legacy uncapped/best-effort JSON/JSONL behavior is unchanged. Existing ambiguous-alias rejection and supported system-temp alias rules are preserved, not weakened into new path adoption. These are mutable filesystem observations, not atomic multi-file snapshots, a filesystem sandbox, native scope identity, independent-process enforcement or proof against hung state I/O. Coordinator/independent-runtime origin ownership, cross-runtime controller authority, supervisor recovery, terminal timing and public source/packed supervision journeys remain open; C and release gates are unchanged.

The real ordinary `startRun` pipeline now offers opt-in kernel `AsyncRunStartOptions.onSupervisionClockCaptured`, separate from Recipe/actor values and all public tool schemas. Only a fresh ordinary generation with this observer captures a genuine monotonic origin; its wall sample creates the same stored `createdAt`/`started_at` and policy cohort. Under the existing lifecycle admission, after fallible launch metadata assembly, it synchronously hands off one frozen `AsyncRunAdmissionClock` containing exact Run/generation/optional owner, state directory, frozen clock, the original origin reference and an opaque original snapshot-reference reader (never raw launch metadata). The observation says `publication: "unpublished"`, `work_started: false`, `visibility: "kernel_only"`; it precedes new-generation metadata, capture setup, native spawn and destructive preparation of prior Run evidence. It is not a launch/status/work/membership receipt, and canonical binding cannot admit that new generation before publication. The caller must retain the exact reference in its own runtime; serializing an origin does not restore continuity. This capture belongs to the caller process, not automatically to an independent supervisor or a restart-recoverable clock service. Normal synchronous callback return values are ignored, not treated as policy or permission. Thrown errors escape unchanged before new work; native Promises/thenables are refused without awaiting under the lock, with native Promise rejection settled to avoid an unhandled rejection. A deferred observer may already have initiated its own side effects; refusal does not cancel/roll them back, and earlier ownership/Recipe-usage effects may also remain. Compatible singleton and legacy reuse return before this hook, preserving original clocks without recapture/backfill; a truly new generation gets a new origin. Prepare-only descendant stages keep their existing unpublished path and emit no ordinary handoff. Fallible metadata assembly (including Recipe-context collection, artifact expansion and transport normalization) now precedes handoff, retained-evidence cleanup and capture acquisition even without a kernel observer. A red witness exposed the prior ordering: an invalid artifact placeholder delivered an origin, removed six prior evidence files and left both capture FDs open despite zero native starts. Two new properties exercise artifact/transport failure across fresh/synthetic-previous layouts and observer/default paths, plus successful artifact preparation before exact observer refusal. Such preparation failure now preserves prior bytes, opens no captures and preserves its exact error without a handoff or native start. These are isolated no-work fixtures, not producer/native-scope proof; later publication/capture/spawn failure cleanup and multi-effect atomicity are not established by this ordering change. With no kernel observer, no origin handoff/controller/reference carrier is introduced. Neither the origin reference, hook nor private monotonic evidence is added to persisted Run metadata or launch output; ordinary actor arguments cannot activate the hook or override policy. Real disposable-runner fixtures cover capture-before-spawn/publication, lifecycle-lock presence, same/legacy/new generation behavior, observer rejection and canonical post-publication use, settling captured producer exit before cleanup. This hook closes admission-time capture/handoff only: default/SDK coordinator ownership acceptance, cross-runtime authority, independent runtime, recovery, terminal timing and public/native feature acceptance remain required. No default controller/timer or public supervision policy is enabled.

Private `createRunSupervisionOwner` in `runs-supervision.ts` now supplies one explicit runtime-local retention/registration lifetime over the existing canonical controller, not an independent supervisor. Its `onSupervisionClockCaptured` callback retains the exact genuine origin, normalized clock and admission root/marker frame without timers or a launched-Run claim. It rejects copied origins and conflicting identity/origin bindings, snapshots data fields without invoking accessors, and preserves the original elapsed allowance. `start(expected)` explicitly admits published canonical state and the original frame before allocating one controller for that identity; repeated calls cannot create another or restart a stopped/faulted controller. `snapshot` performs no IO or clock sampling. `stop` is final; `release` revokes callbacks, removes its retained record and refuses that origin's replay in this owner. Failed launch handoffs remain retained, never successful launch receipts, until explicitly released. Permanent `close` stops retained controllers and preserves known cancellation uncertainty, including failures discovered after reentrant close returns. Reentrant canonical assembly cannot hide a new controller after close. Curated snapshots expose no origin, private path, values or process identity. Real disposable-runner witnesses join the existing handoff to this owner across fresh/singleton/legacy/rejected starts and settle captured runner exit before cleanup; controlled clocks/schedulers exercise pending publication, no renewed allowance, frame replacement and callback revocation. No default owner, timer, public policy, stop actuation or native membership is enabled.

Private `createActorExtensionRuntime(pi, supervisionPorts)` now optionally composes that owner into one coordinator session epoch. The normal `index.ts` factory remains unconfigured: no default owner, public tool parameter, timer, report wake, controller activation or actuation is introduced. The epoch pins the original SDK sessionManager object as well as owner id; event/tool contexts are fresh wrappers, not lifetime identity. Same-manager wrappers may use the original observer, while another manager with the same string id cannot adopt it. Startup captures one epoch before asynchronous preparation but rejects handoffs until ready; only the winning epoch may finish startup. Replacement closes the prior owner, stale callbacks refuse before inspecting capture data, and a trusted shutdown event through a fresh valid same-manager wrapper closes private retention/UI resources. Known invalidated contexts are matched from cached identity without rereading them; an unknown invalidated wrapper cannot authenticate a shutdown. Busy/idle observations and UI callback failure do not themselves revoke the independent private retention epoch. The same synchronous pinned callback is forwarded through ordinary spawn, registered async tools and both internal review launch adapters; ordinary actor values never become that callback. Review callbacks are pinned when their scheduler starts, not retargeted at late launch. No saved-data reconstruction, controller claim/start, deadline notification, native signal or terminal-duration inference occurs here. Host-facade/kernel properties retain their narrower role: overlapping startup and real UI failure are checked independently of retained observations, and a private 1ms ceiling may become due without stop authority. Natural completion still returns timing_unproven. At that private-only checkpoint, legacy parent teardown stayed unchanged: fresh-wrapper private disposal did not repair its cached-context owner lookup or certify delayed same-owner teardown. The separate default-routing repair below does not confer native isolation.

A separate source/packed fixture now composes this private kernel through real public SDK AgentSessionRuntime services, lifecycle events and synthetic-provider tool execution. Four genuine finite Runs (spawn and registered async-tool invocation per layout) exercise fresh tool contexts, busy/idle original observations, persisted same-id resume with a different manager, rejection of another live same-id manager and old hooks, unchanged old-generation clock fields, no DTO/origin reconstruction and disposal through fresh shutdown wrappers. Probe sessions launch no additional Runs. The fixture uses only currently admitted public tool inputs: max_runtime remains refused by that public schema, not silently enabled to make the probe pass. Both runner and workload PIDs are captured before subsequent fixture assertions/awaits, and natural0 plus fresh ESRCH precede SDK teardown and parent deletion; no surviving-workload teardown or owned-scope closure is claimed. Controlled scheduler/deadline ports remain unused. This is private opt-in composition, not the default entrypoint, new public status/reporting or independent/hung-host supervision. Crucially, AgentSessionRuntime emits shutdown before invalidation, while bare AgentSession.dispose only invalidates/disconnects: a raw SDK host must explicitly deliver trusted shutdown beforehand. These journeys do not certify automatic bare-disposal revocation. Supported host-lifetime coverage, default/public routes, ordinary detached-runner frozen timing and cross-runtime recovery remain open.

**Default parent-session teardown routing.** The coordinator now retains a separate original-manager/current-lifetime shutdown binding in the ordinary default path, independent of supervision/completion opt-ins and UI callback failure. A trusted fresh wrapper must match that exact current manager and owner; known current invalidated contexts use cached binding. Old contexts/managers, same-ID replacement, A→B→A, unknown invalidated wrappers and reused retired managers refuse. Shutdown consumes the binding before cleanup and rechecks replacement before continuing into the existing owner/generation-fenced teardown, without callback replay. Existing quit/reload/new/resume/fork semantics stay session-scoped, not newly process-scoped. Property tests cover both default and internal-stream profiles, duplicate callbacks, reentrant wrapper validation, current stale getters and UI failure. A separate real default SDK lifecycle exercises new/resume/fork/quit through actual fresh wrappers and checks newly written exact-owner/trigger teardown summaries, including errors after disposal. It forbids model calls and starts no Actor/native workload: zero attempted candidates proves routing only. Existing parent-teardown properties separately preserve exact generation guards and partial-failure continuation. This neither establishes host-instance isolation between concurrent same-ID hosts, signal/whole-scope termination, independent crash/hung-host supervision nor bare-dispose coverage. B2/C/D native acceptance and original timing/source/stream gates remain open.

**Controlled Linux SDK-quit witness.** A separate source/npm mode of the existing coordinator fixture now loads the real default entrypoint and operating Actors Skill, then publicly spawns two genuine bounded gated command Runs under different SDK session owners. Actual runner handles are captured before await; controlled workload readiness supplies each direct PID. Live command/cwd/start-time frames match captured owner/generation metadata before quit. Disposing the first real SDK runtime while its Run is live produces exactly one matching teardown attempt and one run.kill/SIGKILL event. The captured runner's actual close is signal SIGKILL, not an inferred137 result; fresh ESRCH then verifies runner and direct-workload absence while the owned release gate remains absent. No native close/code/signal is invented for the non-child workload: its outcome is verified absence only. The second owner's runner/workload remain live with unchanged identities through that stop and later settle naturally0 after their own gate release. Both source and actual npm-installed entrypoints replay this witness without private supervision ports, a second spawn provider or external model calls. The parent independently checks raw stdio/actual host close, physical metadata/ready files/reports and all four captured identities per layout, then requires fresh ESRCH for those PIDs plus the host immediately before deleting successful fixture state. Failed checks retain the root and primary/cleanup failures; cleanup releases controlled fixture gates, never retries destructive shutdown. These four genuine Runs per retained source/npm pair are distinct from the older ten-natural-Run coordinator/research journey, which still replays unchanged semantics. Its generated root prefix is shorter solely to keep the full-map witness inside the unchanged16KiB budget under nested TMPDIR; the separate omission witness and all assertions stay intact. This proves the captured ordinary Linux quit case, not complete scope membership/closure, escaped descendants, Windows/macOS, every replacement trigger, crash/hung-host enforcement or independent ownership for parallel same-ID hosts. No production kernel/native backend/policy change follows.

**Three-platform source/packed SDK-quit acceptance.** The same default-entrypoint witness now passed on the actual Linux/macOS/Windows runners in [Validate 42438289689](https://github.com/llblab/pi-actors/actions/runs/42438289689), for candidate `4461d0f`. Each installed job's own checkout records `43c7d56803f3c2914cd133ad3710aa380aeb582e`, whose authenticated tree is `7deafe2c2f7c5fb096df4620817475f7c0359474`. The shutdown case passed in 6154.480984 ms on Ubuntu, 10945.970667 ms on macOS and 39570.4326 ms on Windows. These are whole-case elapsed durations, not cross-process clock alignment or kernel-exit times. Both layouts retain actual platform/command/start-time and runner birth-metadata correspondence; cwd is required on Linux and checked only where supplied elsewhere. POSIX records actual runner SIGKILL close; Windows records numeric exit/null signal with the `taskkill /PID <captured pid> /T /F` request, never a synthesized137 or POSIX close. Surviving foreign-owner identities, absent owned gate, raw stdio/host close and final fresh ESRCH for every captured safe PID remain mandatory. The90s fixture watchdog,180s host timeout,8s absence policy and product limits are unchanged. This accepts only the ordinary captured runner/direct-workload graceful-quit case on those tested CI profiles; descendants, other triggers, concurrent same-ID hosts, crash/hung-host enforcement, complete scope membership/closure and independent owner-instance authority remain open.

**Local integration validation before candidate formation.** Normal npm run validate now completes on the cumulative Linux/Node26 working tree:1596tests/1591pass/5skip, followed by506conformance tests/505pass/1skip, with zero failures. These overlapping populations are not additive. Types, Recipe QA, distributive parity, package checks and dry-pack pass; the separate dependency audit finds zero vulnerabilities. The first full attempt timed out; its buffered stdout was not retained, so no earlier-stage pass is inferred. A durable focused reproduction narrowed a genuine test-process stall to three owned fs.watch handles after all56supervision tests reported success. The intentionally reused controlled context no longer has shutdown authority; cleanup now uses a fresh valid manager lifetime, retaining every startup/observer assertion and explicitly checking close events for owned watches. The process exits naturally, without force-exit/unref or a production change. A separate diagnostic observes zero retained watches after cleanup. The subsequent full product log is retained under /tmp/pi-actors-validation/v-GH5Rrc; later context-only reconciliation has its own validation. This closes the current local validation slice, not A/B/C integration, loaded UI acceptance, native macOS/Windows CI, owner-loss enforcement, version selection or D3 candidate acceptance. No commit, publication, reload or operator Run action occurs.

Participating owners sharing one exact genuine origin in this module/runtime now coordinate through a private weak claim. Activation reserves it before canonical assembly; a contender receives `controller_claimed` while another owner's controller or provisional assembly holds it. Stop alone does not transfer an allocated controller's claim: explicit release/close revokes its callbacks before handover. Failed assembly drops only its own reservation; a late nonholder cleanup cannot erase another owner's claim. The origin's first identity/state/frame binding persists across owner release, preventing copied-state adoption. Handover observes the original elapsed allowance, never a new current-time budget. This is cooperative logical ownership, not proof that an old physical timer was removed, exactly-once due notification across successive controllers or global scope authority. Distinct origins, module instances, processes and unregistered low-level controller constructors are outside that claim. Real launch handoff and controlled assembly/handover witnesses cover this boundary; cross-runtime authority, independent supervision/recovery, ordinary detached-runner frozen timing and public/native acceptance remain open.

A compatible singleton reuse retains its original anchor, deadline, and report schedule. Compare normalized effective policies, so `60s` and `1m` are equivalent; conflicting policies fail closed instead of retiming a live singleton. A genuinely new generation receives new clocks. Running policies are not mutable through `message` in this increment.

New metadata records `createdAt` and `started_at` from the same generation-admission anchor. The stored supervision fields form a coherent cohort: positive integer milliseconds or explicit null, a canonical ISO start, and exactly the deadline implied by that start and ceiling. Partial, coercible, or contradictory clocks fail closed rather than being repaired. A pre-supervision generation with wholly absent clock fields stays explicitly unconfigured; it can be reused under disabled policies without rewriting its metadata, but cannot acquire a new allowance or today's Recipe policy merely by reattachment.

## Runtime-Owned Supervision

Supervision belongs to the runtime outside the actor's workload callback. It must remain effective while the coordinator is busy/idle, a child command hangs, or the actor emits no Trace. The UI/delivery scheduler does not own termination, and missing model-context acceptance cannot extend an execution deadline.

Intervals are eligibility thresholds, not hard-real-time delivery promises. The first diagnostic becomes eligible after `report_every`; later eligibility follows the last accepted diagnostic delivery plus the interval. While a report is pending, refresh its latest snapshot instead of enqueueing every elapsed tick. Reattachment produces at most one currently due snapshot, never a catch-up burst. No configured interval means no periodic model wakeups.

In addition to each Run's interval, the exact top-level coordinator session admits routine diagnostic batches no more often than once per `60000ms`. This is one shared gate for all its Runs and containing trees, not a separate allowance for every Run, child session, or staggered timer. Both floors use `RUN_DIAGNOSTIC_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` from the policy owner; the gate is a delivery ceiling, not an implicit reporting default. It may postpone diagnostics further than their per-Run eligibility.

Advance the shared cooldown only from accepted routine diagnostic delivery, not a proposed packet, ordinary completion, explicit urgent `steer`, or manual `inspect`. An admitted packet with unresolved acceptance retains its one diagnostic admission slot without spawning additional diagnostic packets. Preserve/recover the gate with exact owner and acceptance evidence across busy/idle transitions and reattachment; uncertain timing/acceptance defers routine diagnostics rather than inventing a fresh slot. At the next eligible boundary, merge the latest due snapshots fairly instead of replaying missed intervals. Independent top-level sessions retain independent gates.

Terminal outcomes and explicit urgent semantic attention bypass the routine diagnostic cooldown, while retaining their existing safe-boundary, budget, lineage, and exact-acceptance rules. An execution deadline never waits for this gate. Manual inspection neither consumes nor resets it, and ordinary completion delivery never depends on enabling periodic diagnostics or execution limits.

Keep timers tied to the exact owner, logical Run id, and `run_instance_id`. Recheck authoritative lifecycle/process evidence before freezing a diagnostic or applying termination. Old timers cannot affect a replacement generation.

### Deadline and Terminal Truth

`deadline_at` begins the canonical stop procedure, not an optimistic terminal declaration. Under the lifecycle lock, revalidate ownership, generation, process identity, and current terminal evidence, record the reason `max_runtime`, and stop admission of further owned work. Use the existing verified termination/escalation path for the workload and attached descendants whose exact parent-generation lineage is proven. Never kill by remembered PID, shared working directory, or logical Run id alone.

- A terminal outcome accepted before the deadline stop claim wins and is delivered normally.
- A deadline claim accepted while the generation is running owns the eventual timeout reason, even if the process subsequently exits with code zero during shutdown.
- Add `timed_out` as a terminal Run status only after process termination and owned-tree cleanup are confirmed. Extend all terminal readers, collectors, renderers, and retention guards consistently; a leaf command's existing `timeout` is not automatically a Run-level deadline.
- Until termination is proven, retain a truthful nonterminal status with `phase: "stopping"` and bounded stop-failure diagnostics. Sending a signal or exhausting a wait does not prove death.
- Persist one generation-bound terminal outcome. Preserve accepted publications, captures, and useful partial artifacts; mark unfinished task output partial. Termination does not roll back filesystem or external side effects and does not authorize automatic retry.

Natural-result producers bind once to the kernel-published PID, owner, and generation under the lifecycle lock before executing work. Native command preparation and every attempt/retry must serialize admission against the same stop claim, not use an unlocked check-then-spawn. If admission rejects a later retry, retain already settled attempt receipts/captures, mark the command interrupted, and clear active accounting without assigning a native exit to the unspawned attempt. Prepare capture resources before spawning; the executor must manage stream/exit/error handling before exposing a synchronous kernel-only managed-attempt callback inside launch admission. This callback carries the retained fresh handle, not native birth/identity proof. The private birth binder must consume the exact runtime-local executor admission captured before spawn plus its fresh managed context under the same producer/generation lifecycle admission. Copied or late contexts, different nested factories, retroactive adoption, native contradictions, or failed acquisition/persistence cannot authorize a replacement binding. Retain a write-once observed PID/native identity on its intent only after acquisition; this timestamped observation is neither current liveness nor complete scope membership. Existing intent-only records remain unresolved, and adding birth observations never releases the replacement/retirement fence. Direct-member settlement requires the exact retained birth/intent and authentic synchronous native-close receipt, checked before lifecycle acquisition and revalidated under scoped evidence authority. Rejected fabricated/copied receipts must not consume later genuine settlement permission; only an accepted close/member correspondence consumes the recording attempt before lock/storage admission. Persist immutable observed exit code or signal; never normalize a null native exit to success or synthesize a Run kill cause. Evidence may be recorded while stopping or after accepted natural result without changing root truth. Already recorded settlements can survive producer death, but stored data is not a runtime capability. Private evidence-only recovery may store `{basis: "verified_absence", observed_at: "<canonical ISO>"}` only for a retained birth after captured producer exit and member ESRCH, under exact generation/owner/fingerprint/birth revalidation. It never supplies unobserved exit code/signal, repairs missing identity from a bare PID, changes root outcome/cause/clock, or dispatches a signal. Existing settlement wins without reprobe; unsupported/denied/live/mismatched evidence remains unproven. Missing native-close details stay unknown, and even every recorded member settling cannot establish exhaustive membership or release the scope fence. A post-spawn observer/admission failure retains captures and the first error until the managed process settles, then rejects without retry or rollback. Revoke the single-use factory when its synchronous admission window ends, including refusal. Capture-write failures must remain inside stream management, preserve bounded tails/draining and the first error, and explicitly mark the attempt stream incomplete with physical saved bytes separate from bytes observed (unknown saved bytes are null). A known-PID handle error alone is not an exit; retain management until close. Persist observed settled attempts and command interruption through the producer's generation/owner/native-fingerprint-fenced evidence port independently of ordinary result publication. Stopping does not forbid factual evidence writes; preserve accepted root status and do not infer terminal or work authority from a receipt. Before actuation, separately retain launch/membership evidence and recover unobserved settlement/interruption when runner death precedes its receipt. The private intent-only admission primitive (`native-launches.json`, `run-native-launches-v1`) retains owner/generation/runner binding and command-attempt admission before a factory; it is not installed in ordinary runners yet. Neither an absent journal nor a throwing factory proves no native child, and the journal never proves complete membership. Lifecycle cleanup exceptions must not replace the exact factory return/throw: expose release uncertainty separately and fence further producer operations; absence of a cleanup diagnostic does not prove lock release. Any retained intent-only journal, including corrupt, foreign, nonregular, empty, or unreadable evidence, blocks replacement and archive/prune before state mutation; natural acceptance or runner exit does not release that fence. Compatible active singleton attachment keeps the same generation and clock without reopening scope. Wire ordinary runners only after positive scope closure/recovery and live birth/member binding; reject incompatible or unknown persisted fields instead of erasing evidence. Separately spawned child Runs still need their own generation-bound admission and membership proof. Natural-result acceptance and stop claims use that same lock; after waiting, revalidate the captured identity instead of adopting the latest generation. Keep an accepted result immutable, and stamp its generation/acceptance time in the runtime rather than trusting workload-provided metadata. This local result publication does not by itself establish forced whole-tree termination. After an accepted stop claim, retain a later workload result as an explicitly nonterminal capture without a natural-success/failure terminal Trace or handled marker. Runner exit or a complete workload payload cannot authorize reuse while the claimed owned scope remains unproven.

An unavailable or denied OS liveness probe is not evidence of absence, and an identity mismatch alone is not death of the captured execution. A single-process exit observation is only one member of stop proof: it does not establish process-group/tree cleanup or the termination of separately owned descendant Runs. A POSIX group absence probe likewise establishes only one absence fact, not group ownership, complete membership, or separately owned descendant-Run cleanup. Session-owner discovery and delivery ancestry cannot substitute for that control authority. Aggregate the complete proven scope before finalizing a stop.

No new user-facing grace-period setting is introduced. Termination uses the existing platform policy; `max_runtime` is an execution cutoff followed by verified cleanup, not a guarantee of instantaneous OS process death. Shutdown and parent teardown keep their existing authority and cannot be disabled by null supervision fields.

Status, control and producer-evidence readers share regular-file, generation-bound natural/capture validation. Status precedence and the rendered result use one parsed result snapshot, so an earlier accepted natural result remains `done`/`failed` after later malformed or matching pending stop evidence, without a synthetic stopping cause or file changes. Stop-request ACKs likewise prefer accepted natural evidence before reading later stop records, after exact generation/owner/protocol checks and without native probes or file changes. Invalid/captured/unbound data cannot acquire that priority; pending stop claims keep their nonterminal ACK. Supported legacy result records remain display-compatible without gaining accepted-natural authority. This fixes result projection, not native-scope closure or public supervision enforcement.

## Owned-Scope Proof Gate

This is bounded implementation evidence, not a new public API or approval of a production containment backend.

[`startRun`](../lib/async-runs.ts) protects the new Run's own state and launches a detached runner. Spawn and async registered tools use that path, but actual-spawning-member admission and authenticated cross-owner descendant membership are still absent. [Delivery lineage](../lib/run-delivery-lineage.ts) routes completions; [parent-session teardown](../lib/runs-parent-teardown.ts) filters exact session owners. Neither is descendant control authority. [Ordinary native attempts](../scripts/async-runner.mjs) still use `withRunWorkAdmission`, not the private native intent/birth/settlement ports.

Private [child intent/preparation persistence](../lib/runs-descendant-launches.ts) retains declared targets in `descendant-launches.json` (`run-descendant-launches-v1`), not control capabilities. The kernel-only `prepareDescendantRunGeneration` shares `startRun` metadata creation but requires fresh state: it allocates the real child generation under parent-then-child lifecycle locks into unpublished `prepared-run.json`, never canonical `run.json`/progress, native spawn, captures, Recipe launch usage, `run.start`, or a started-Run receipt. This avoids an unstarted preparation becoming an ordinary `exited` Run or terminal candidate and preserves earlier evidence rather than replacing it. Before committing the parent's optional `preparation` fact, it records `descendant-preparation.json` (`run-descendant-preparation-v1`) with reciprocal exact-generation references and `parent_producer` (the original admitted PID/captured fingerprint, explicit null if unavailable), then revalidates the callback-scoped genuine parent plus current staged child metadata. This binding never comes from actor values or refreshed parent metadata. Caller UUIDs, delivery lineage, copied producer data, self aliases and live singleton substitution cannot supply that generation. Child and parent writes are not one atomic transaction: failures retain available evidence, never imply no birth elsewhere, and do not retry or resume implicitly. Known child-release failure preserves the original action outcome, quarantines the parent producer, and retains the first cleanup cause, not proof of released locks. Any staged metadata, marker, or parent journal stays fenced against replacement/archive/prune, even when later writes fail; missing records are not closure proof, and ordinary live singleton attachment still preserves its generation/clock.

Same-runtime `reconcileRunDescendantPreparation` can explicitly repair a missing parent fact or add preparation monotonically to its exact declared intent, preserving id/admission time/target, child files, root results and budgets. It requires the genuine producer's evidence permission, even while stopping or after natural acceptance; a new fact revalidates parent identity after child-lock contention plus exact unpublished staged evidence and original producer binding. Legacy-unbound or foreign producer references remain readable data but cannot create/upgrade a prepared fact or backfill identity. Already accepted facts win unchanged without child probes. Each failed I/O call ends without implicit retry; a later explicit call may make fresh observations. This establishes metadata evidence, not native birth, authenticated workload-spawner/live membership, complete scope, lock release, or power-loss recovery. The stored binding is reference evidence, not native identity acquisition or reconstructed permission.

Private `recoverRunDescendantPreparation` now admits evidence-only parent repair after producer loss: it matches exact parent/child references and original PID/fingerprint to supported saved parent identity, then requires a fresh ESRCH-only absence observation. It revalidates under parent-then-child lifecycle admission after contention and observations; absent/unbound/unsupported/reused identity, changed metadata and published child state remain refused or unproven. No producer capability, native exit code/signal, work permission or scope closure is reconstructed. Root result/cause/progress/clock and child files stay byte-preserved, including opaque root evidence. Accepted facts reuse without child/native probes. Explicit calls do not retry failed persistence internally. Cleanup preserves the primary outcome and first release failure, with runtime-local recovery-scope quarantine; durable quarantine recovery, incomplete preparations and whole-scope closure remain unimplemented. Ordinary child starts remain unwired.

The local native Linux evidence establishes these separate boundaries:

- **Group absence is not descendant closure.** A disposable leader exited and its original group returned `ESRCH` while a `setsid` descendant remained nonterminal and non-waitable. The fixture subsequently reaped it.
- **Resource emptiness is not historical ownership.** A cooperative cgroup-v2 fixture retained its detached descendant until exit. A separate same-UID migration fixture moved a descendant from source A to sibling B: the leader/process group disappeared, but source/destination/outer-parent population was `[0,1,1]` while the descendant lived. The outer parent stayed populated, so this does not contradict recursive cgroup accounting.
- **One namespace boundary rejected one migration path.** With `nsdelegate`, equal control-file modes/ownership and matching host-visible UID/GID/groups, no-new-namespace and user-only controls allowed A→B. The joint user+cgroup namespace case rejected that path-based migration with `ENOENT`, leaving population `[1,0,1]`. Namespace inheritance was verified; descriptor escape, arbitrary namespace escape and production integration were not.
- **Primary-ID restoration is not transparent group compatibility.** A three-case Node `v26.8.1` probe restored UID/EUID/GID/EGID, user lookup and private-workspace owner IDs by mapping only the caller's own UID/primary GID. Fixture-file access succeeded and joint-namespace migration stayed denied. However, a non-primary baseline group's virtual ID still differed, despite unchanged group count and host-visible UID/GID/groups. This does not prove lost kernel membership or lost group-based filesystem access.
- **The tested unprivileged full-group request was refused.** One fresh child attempted exactly one `gid_map` write containing two single-ID ranges for its already-existing primary/supplementary groups, after its own UID mapping and private `setgroups=deny`. The write returned `EPERM`; `gid_map` remained empty. No retry, privileged helper, account change or host-policy change followed. This bounds that request in this environment, not every possible architecture.

Recorded successful fixture cleanup reaped the held descendants, removed only fixture-owned resources/workspaces, and left caller namespaces/cgroup/host credentials unchanged. Those observations are not arbitrary-failure cleanup, controller-loss recovery, statistical corroboration, or native Darwin/Windows proof.

The namespace candidate remains parked; the strict native direction adopted below does not adopt this namespace mechanism or authorize another mapping experiment. No security sandbox, mapping helper, production backend, blanket elevated-privilege requirement or host-policy change is selected. Further design must preserve the complete-owned-tree contract and unchanged workload credentials rather than silently accepting credential drift or weaker closure.

Before positive whole-scope closure, establish trusted generation-bound resource identity, seal every native/child-Run admission path, account for recursive exact-generation child closure and unresolved launches, and preserve immutable evidence. All recorded members settling is still insufficient. Unknown authority remains unproven; PID/PGID presence, delivery ancestry or a resource path cannot substitute. Native scope authority, ordinary native/child journal wiring and retained-journal retirement remain gated by [Checkpoint C](../BACKLOG.md). Checkpoints A/B may advance without a production native backend; public feature ingress waits for its runtime readiness, and positive deadline acceptance plus all agreed branches must pass Checkpoint D before the single release.

### Owner-Loss Termination: Approved Outcome and Mechanism Gate

The operator approves the outcome and now adopts the strict native direction: an independent native controller with separately prepared authority over protected per-platform scopes, preserving workload UID/GID/supplementary groups and refusing silent best-effort fallback. The instruction to record the direction and do the necessary work authorizes scoped development toward prerelease, not a claim that a backend exists. Linux, macOS and Windows remain the intended platform set; macOS/Windows native execution is available through the existing Validate CI matrix, not this Linux workstation. Concrete privilege changes, service installation, credential/account/host-policy changes, release and publication still need their own admission. A native helper role is approved; its implementation, packaging, precise authority and each OS mechanism remain to be established. No production owner-loss watcher, metadata schema, native API or public switch is implemented by recording this decision.

**Current evidence and missing authority.** The ordinary start metadata has logical ownerId plus the runner's pid/process_identity; these are not the Pi host's birth identity. The original SDK-manager/current-lifetime binding fixes trusted graceful shutdown and stale callbacks inside its coordinator, not independent observation of host death. The controlled source/packed quit witnesses prove only their captured runner/direct-workload cases on the tested Linux/macOS/Windows CI profiles, not complete membership, crash supervision or whole-scope closure. A fixture's captured SDK-host frame is test evidence, not a production owner capability. Session ids, copied history, PID/PGID integers, paths, environment, heartbeat files and serialized DTOs must not mint or reconstruct that capability.

**Owner instance, not just session id or PID.** The required conceptual owner is a trusted Pi host lifetime together with its logical-session epoch. It is not a proposed serialized schema. Several session epochs may share a host process; reopening/importing the same session id does not adopt another live instance's Runs. The trusted quit/reload/new/resume/fork path keeps its existing epoch-revocation semantics; /tree does not itself end that epoch. A physical host death may end several of its admitted epochs, but cannot authorize stopping an independently live host with the same session id. File-research permission after resume remains separate from live-work adoption.

**Required lifecycle before ordinary activation.**

- Retain genuine host-birth/lifetime authority at trusted composition, with an independent observer that can survive loss of that host. Pin it before workload birth; do not acquire a replacement identity after loss.
- Bind each exact Run/root/generation to that original owner instance and its already-authoritative native scope before any work can execute. The bind/spawn failure windows must preserve uncertainty; no unbound work or inferred historical adoption.
- Authenticate native and separately spawned descendant-Run membership at ingress, including cross-owner descendants. Existing delivery ancestry, discovery/catalog rows and declared/staged child targets do not supply this authority.
