- Treat a trusted epoch revocation or proven original host exit as a stop trigger. A missed heartbeat, delayed callback, UI failure, slow model or SIGSTOP alone is not positive owner death. Runtime deadlines retain their own clock/cause rules, not an invented owner-loss timeout.
- Revalidate the exact owner-instance/Run/scope relation at actuation, seal further ingress and preserve natural/first-cause arbitration. PID reuse, A→B→A, replacement, duplicate callbacks and supervisor reconnect cannot revive an old grant.
- Confirm the complete admitted native/child scope before terminal finalization or guard release. A Kill attempt, runner close, captured-member settlements or an empty old process group is insufficient for escaped descendants.
- Keep malformed/legacy-unbound state and lost observer authority explicit and fenced. Do not rebuild permission from journals, reset clocks, migrate old packets, signal guessed PIDs or retry destructive work to obtain a cleaner receipt. No minimally implied keep-alive/transfer exception is added.

**Existing ownership seams.** extension-runtime owns trusted host/session composition; the async-runs admission facade and existing lifecycle/ownership domains own exact-generation admission; the supervision/controller owners own independent observation and stop arbitration; existing native/descendant owners own membership and closure evidence. These are responsibilities, not approved new API signatures or a second registry. Any earned OS adapter must follow the local DAG and keep actor configuration/data outside its authority ports.

**Adopted direction; mechanism proof remains open.** Prefer OS-enforced native scopes behind the existing lifecycle/supervision owners, not a PID-tree scanner, process-group-only watcher or second global registry. Retain the original owner lifetime and exact Run generation before work; let the independent controller survive Pi loss, seal admission, request native stop and positively establish complete scope closure. Protect the controller's authority from workload-controlled mutation within the explicitly established threat boundary; a separate process alone is not a security boundary. This does not adopt a new native API, arbitrary same-UID security sandbox, clock carrier or public activation.

- **Linux preferred candidate:** pidfd-based original-host observation plus a protected cgroup-v2 subtree and cgroup.kill where available. Prove the actual delegation/control boundary and deny migration outside the owned scope; a writable same-UID sibling invalidates a naive emptiness claim. Prepared delegation or a narrowly authorized system broker may be necessary; never run Pi/workloads as root by implication or alter their credential/group view. Availability of Linux or a delegated user subtree alone is insufficient. The parked namespace/group-compatibility experiments remain parked.
- **Windows preferred candidate:** original process HANDLE plus Job Objects, assigning the workload before it executes, with appropriate no-breakaway/nested-job policy and controlled handle ownership. TerminateJobObject and KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE have distinct roles; a retained/duplicated handle changes last-close behavior. Ordinary creation of one's own processes/jobs commonly needs no administrator, but existing enclosing Jobs, access rights and program compatibility must be checked. External-service launches such as WMI Win32_Process.Create do not automatically join the Job; authenticated descendant-Run ingress remains required.
- **macOS feasibility first:** establish an actually supported native scope mechanism before implementing the common backend path. kqueue exit observation is not containment; process-group/launchd group cleanup does not cover escaped descendants. Apple's current XNU event header marks NOTE_TRACK/NOTE_TRACKERR/NOTE_CHILD unsupported since10.5, so their recursive-fork tracking cannot be assumed. Elevated helper rights alone do not identify a complete scope primitive. Evaluate supported mechanisms and their exact authority, ingress/escape and compatibility boundaries; expose an unresolved mechanism as a gate, not a weaker macOS implementation. A Linux guest/VM on macOS is a different execution environment, not native Darwin acceptance, and requires a separate product decision.

Source-level references for this direction are the [Linux cgroup-v2 delegation/containment and cgroup.kill contract](https://docs.kernel.org/admin-guide/cgroup-v2.html), [Microsoft Job Objects](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/procthread/job-objects), [Apple XNU event flags](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/main/bsd/sys/event.h) and [Apple launchd process-group cleanup description](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/launchd/blob/main/man/launchd.plist.5). These support limits/candidate selection, not native acceptance on the installed OS or proof of production compatibility. Further scoped preparation/development is permitted; actual privilege/setup operations and any relaxation of scope, workload compatibility or the three-platform promise remain separately gated.

**macOS source-feasibility result; native proof still absent.** The bounded source pass used Apple XNU revision [f6217f891ac0](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/tree/f6217f891ac0bb64f3d375211650a4c1ff8ca1ea), not execution on macOS or proof that a CI runner uses that exact kernel. It identified a concrete authority/bootstrap gate, not a proof that all macOS architectures are impossible.

- **Coalitions are a conditional kernel candidate, not a selected backend.** In this snapshot, [coalition syscall dispatch](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/f6217f891ac0bb64f3d375211650a4c1ff8ca1ea/bsd/kern/sys_coalition.c) checks task_is_in_privileged_coalition before create/terminate/reap; root UID alone does not satisfy that predicate. [Spawn binding](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/f6217f891ac0bb64f3d375211650a4c1ff8ca1ea/bsd/kern/kern_exec.c) checks privileged-coalition membership or a coalition-spawn entitlement for explicitly supplied coalitions. The [header installation rules](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/f6217f891ac0bb64f3d375211650a4c1ff8ca1ea/bsd/sys/Makefile) classify coalition.h with Apple-internal private headers/XNU exports, not the ordinary public DATAFILES. The resource model has active accounting and termination state, but coalition_terminate requests lifecycle termination/empty notification, not a whole-scope signal: its syscall comment explicitly permits existing members to fork, and [the implementation](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/f6217f891ac0bb64f3d375211650a4c1ff8ca1ea/osfmk/kern/coalition.c) marks termination once active_count reaches zero. No supported third-party launchd/controller bootstrap, sealed complete-membership kill protocol or compatible recovery path was established. Neither the API name, readable coalition id, root helper nor debug unrestrict_coalition_syscalls setting supplies those missing grants; do not change boot/SIP policy to obtain them.
- **EndpointSecurity is not sufficient containment by itself.** Apple's [client contract](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/endpointsecurity/es_new_client(_:_:)) requires its entitlement and user TCC/Full Disk Access approval; the [initialization result](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/endpointsecurity/es_new_client_result_t) also distinguishes not-running-as-root. The documented event enumeration includes NOTIFY_FORK, not an AUTH_FORK gate; AUTH_EXEC alone does not gate a forked child that executes work without exec. Apple's [global sequence contract](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/endpointsecurity/es_message_t/global_seq_num) explicitly accounts for dropped events. Such telemetry may aid a separately proven mechanism, but does not itself supply inherited scope confinement, membership retained before work or authoritative complete closure. No entitlement/FDA setup or ES implementation is adopted.
- **Audit-session identity is not an immutable scope capability.** The inspected [audit-session implementation](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/f6217f891ac0bb64f3d375211650a4c1ff8ca1ea/bsd/security/audit/audit_session.c) supports joining via Mach session ports and updates audit credentials; obtaining another session's port is separately privileged. The reviewed interface did not establish an irreversible per-Run admission seal, authoritative whole-session kill or complete closure. Do not substitute an ASID or move the workload into another audit context without a compatibility/authority contract.
- **App Sandbox is not a transparent lifecycle backend.** Apple's [supported overview](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/security/app-sandbox) describes restricting resources through entitlements. Applying a new sandbox would change ordinary workload access and needs a separate execution-compatibility decision; this overview supplies no per-Run scope-kill/closure contract. This pass does not adopt sandbox-exec profiles, a VM/guest substitution or arbitrary-process security sandboxing.

The next admissible macOS mechanism needs a concrete supported controller/bootstrap path (or an explicitly accepted bounded SPI/OS integration contract), a pre-work native scope identity, actual fork/setsid and external-service ingress rules, stop/seal plus positive complete-emptiness/recovery semantics, and an explicit supported macOS/SDK matrix. Native-CI falsifiers must cover ordinary versus required controller authority, creation/binding failure before work, same-UID unrelated controls, fork/setsid membership, ingress/stop races, controller/owner loss, identity reuse and actual settled captured processes. A denied syscall or observed fork counter alone cannot pass C1. **Supported OS-manager bootstrap check.** Apple's [SMAppService contract](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/servicemanagement/smappservice) controls helper executables inside an app's main bundle and is available on macOS13+. Agent/daemon plist names must refer to the corresponding bundle LaunchAgents/LaunchDaemons directories. [Registration](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/servicemanagement/smappservice/register()) is subject to user approval; a daemon is not bootstrapped until administrator approval, and registration has subsequent-login/boot semantics rather than an automatic Pi-owner-death lease. This identifies deployment/consent requirements, not native scope authority; no app-bundle distribution, minimum-OS policy or service installation is adopted for the current npm extension.

[Unregistration](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/servicemanagement/smappservice/unregister()) promises to stop the running helper service and prevent later launches. Neither that contract nor its completion callback supplies an exclusive per-Run coalition handle, controller coalition privileges, sealed descendant admission or positive closure of escaped descendants. The older [SMJobRemove contract](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/servicemanagement/smjobremove(_:_:_:_:_:)) optionally waits for the job process to exit, not an expressly guaranteed complete-owned-scope proof; SMJobSubmit/Remove are deprecated on macOS since10.10. Do not introduce that legacy path as a new guaranteed fallback. The previously inspected launchd process-group cleanup description remains only group cleanup, not a missing whole-scope contract. As a supplementary CLI reference, the reviewed [Xcode launchctl manual mirror](https://keith.github.io/xcode-man-pages/launchctl.1.html) describes bootstrap/bootout as service/domain definition operations and kill as sending a signal to a running service; it explicitly warns that print output is not API and procinfo output is diagnostic-only. Parsing those diagnostics cannot supply a stable scope-identity contract. This mirror is not installed-runner evidence. The overall result is a source-contract limit: it does not assert what an unobserved macOS launchd build internally does or prove that every OS-manager integration is impossible.

The bounded public OS-manager question is now resolved to a gap: the reviewed supported interfaces do not establish the exclusive scope/issuer/lifetime relation needed by C1. A successful registration, job label, PID, unregister callback or helper exit cannot be promoted into that relation. Further implementation needs a concrete confirmed OS authority/scope contract, or an explicitly bounded macOS SPI/CI feasibility contract naming the private interfaces, supported OS/SDK matrix, actual issuer/bootstrap, required job/privilege setup, capture/cleanup and falsifiers. That would still be investigation, not production adoption or evidence of success. No private authority, service/boot/SIP change or weaker execution semantics follows from these source observations. With no new concrete mechanism/authority input, stop rather than repeat either source scan or manufacture a prototype around an assumed scope.

#### macOS SPI and CI Feasibility Plan

**Status and admission.** Both A and B are recorded and priority-reviewed. The operator subsequently admitted authoring A and, separately, a metadata-only inventory mode in the same manual workflow: the first-profile fixture/harness, inventory collection wiring and non-native authoring checks are prepared below. Neither macOS inventory nor Darwin SPI/native fixture execution has been admitted or performed by those authoring checkpoints. B is not implemented. Neither this authoring admission nor recording/review permits native execution, CI launch, job/service registration, rights changes, push, or Grow Loop resumption. A is a deliberately narrow observational pilot; B remains a conditional authority/closure investigation. Neither PASS_A nor any research-job completion closes C1, enables a backend, selects a clock carrier, or supplies source/packed product acceptance.

**Priority order.** Check authority and cleanup safety first, then ABI/profile admission, then observation quality and matrix coverage. Do not widen a failing pilot to new APIs, privileges or OS versions to obtain a green result.

**Candidate interfaces and non-authority.** A initially needs only proc_pidinfo with PROC_PIDCOALITIONINFO and PROC_PIDUNIQIDENTIFIERINFO, bound to the fixture's positively captured processes. Pin the reviewed declarations to [XNU f6217f891ac0 and its private proc-info header](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/xnu/blob/f6217f891ac0bb64f3d375211650a4c1ff8ca1ea/bsd/sys/proc_info_private.h); verify full declared ABI/return size and field semantics rather than guessing a pair of uint64 values. Preserve reserved fields/layout; do not fetch latest headers, patch system headers or reinterpret unknown bytes in CI. Preserve native uint64 identities losslessly as canonical unsigned decimal strings in research artifacts, never rounded JavaScript Numbers or unchecked signed casts; retain the reviewed coalition type/index mapping. A readable symbol, coalition id, birth/version DTO or matching hash is observation, not a retained native control capability. coalition_info_resource_usage is not required by A: aggregate accounting may describe a shared CI coalition and does not establish membership completeness or closure. Coalition create/terminate/reap and private coalition spawn attributes are B candidates only, with no selected userspace binding or admitted caller authority.

**Research matrix, not a product support policy.** Use Node24 to match current validation and native, non-Rosetta execution on these proposed profiles, as listed by the reviewed [runner-image catalog](https://github.com/actions/runner-images#available-images):

- **First pilot:** macos-15 / arm64, Xcode16.4 build16F6, macOS SDK15.5.
- **After a reviewable first witness:** macos-26 / arm64, Xcode26.6 build17F113, macOS SDK26.5.
- **Architecture check:** macos-15-intel / x86_64, Xcode16.4 build16F6, macOS SDK15.5.

Use a job-local DEVELOPER_DIR, never sudo xcode-select or SDK installation. Labels do not pin immutable images. Before private calls, compare actual OS/build/architecture, ImageVersion, Xcode/build, SDK, compiler and reviewed fixture ABI/profile against the explicitly approved experiment profile; persist the exact tuple plus source/binary hashes. Missing labels/toolchains, unexpected root execution or unreviewed image/profile drift yield ENVIRONMENT_BLOCKED before workload admission, not silent substitution or a new product minimum-OS rule. The runner manifests are catalog evidence, not execution on those images.

**A — bounded read-only native SPI pilot.** First-profile source authoring is admitted and prepared; native execution still needs separate admission. Later matrix rows remain behind first-witness review and their own profile admission.

1. **CI isolation:** use an opt-in, non-publishing research run on an exact approved commit with contents-read-only permission, no secrets, fork-triggered privileged execution or pull_request_target. Do not add this research result as a replacement for the normal three-OS product validation. The normal product Validate workflow remains unchanged.
2. **Ordinary rights:** preserve numeric UID/EUID/GID/EGID and supplementary groups. No sudo, entitlement, Full Disk Access, service/job registration, private spawn binding, coalition mutation, SIP/boot change or actual operator Pi/Run is involved. Do not enumerate unrelated system processes or dump the CI environment.
3. **Finite topology:** one dedicated driver and two independent fixture branches A/B, each with one short-lived child: at most five fixture processes including the driver, outside the test harness. The unrelated control is fixture-owned too. Direct parents capture positive birth/PID before waits/assertions, retain birth/version correspondence and publish bounded readiness frames. The outer harness retains the driver's real child handle, raw stdio and actual close; the driver cannot self-certify its exit.
4. **Observation cases:** ABI/access results; own captured-PID identity and coalition membership; controlled fork/setsid before/after observations; two-branch comparison; natural gated settlement. A does not deliberately kill an owner/controller, inject stop races or claim ingress confinement. Shared coalition ids across branches are a legitimate result and a veto on adopting that inherited coalition as an exclusive Run scope, not failure of a correctly recorded observational pilot.
5. **Lifecycle and budgets:** fixtures remain gated until frames are captured; each workload has a finite30s watchdog, the host bound is180s and the supported absence-observation window is8s. These are research bounds only, not product-budget changes or proof of exit. On frame-persistence or readiness failure keep gates closed and retain known effects/frames; do not invent missing births or add a workload to repair evidence.
6. **Cleanup:** normal cleanup releases only the owned fixture gates; each actual parent reaps its direct children. Preserve actual waitpid/close code/signal only where observed; non-child absence has no fabricated exit code. Before deleting scratch require the relevant actual settlements and fresh ESRCH for every positively captured process, including the driver observed by its outer parent. PID mismatch/reuse, live/EPERM/unknown proof, timeout, harness loss or missing birth/settlement retains the root and ordered primary/cleanup faults. A never uses coalition kill, pkill, PID-group scans or read-check-then-kill(pid) as a fallback. CI VM disposal is not the fixture's closure proof.

**A authoring checkpoint — not a native witness.** Test-only [C driver](../tests/fixtures/macos-spi-pilot.c), [shared C transport core](../tests/fixtures/macos-spi-transport.h) and [Node harness](../tests/fixtures/macos-spi-pilot.mjs) are outside the distributive and import no runtime owner. The existing source/packed infrastructure test owner holds the synthetic parser/profile/gate/report/absence properties and the opt-in C-unit below. Importing the harness does not run commands, read files or probe native processes. For this pilot, ordinary validation runs the JS properties and explicitly skips the compiler cohort unless admitted by its test-only flag; no compiler dependency or public tool/Recipe/default path is added. The product Validate workflow remains unchanged; the separate manual research workflow below is authoring-only.

The first implementation supports only macos-15/arm64, Node24, Xcode16.4 build16F6, SDK15.5. The explicit native CLI, **not executed by this checkpoint**, takes three arguments: --execute-readonly-pilot-a, the reviewed profile JSON path and /Applications/Xcode_16.4.app/Contents/Developer. It requires ordinary-user Darwin manual CI (CI=true and GITHUB_EVENT_NAME=workflow_dispatch), a clean exact approved commit matching GITHUB_SHA, and the exact profile before compiling/running the driver. The [manual research workflow](../.github/workflows/macos-spi-pilot-a.yml) is now authored, not dispatched. Its execution still requires separate approval of the exact commit and reviewed v2 profile, contents-read-only permissions, no secrets and a finite outer job deadline. Authoring and passing local guard tests are not that approval.

The closed profile schema is pi-actors-spi-a-profile-v2 with string fields schema, profile, platform, arch, node_major, os_version, os_build, darwin_release, image_version, xcode_version, xcode_build, sdk_version, sdk_build, compiler_version, developer_dir, github_repository, commit, fixture_sha256, transport_sha256 and harness_sha256. The new transport_sha256 binds the compiled header as well as the driver and harness; v1/missing-header profiles refuse, never silently upgrade. The actual tuple is measured and retained; it is never automatically promoted to the approved tuple in the same job. Hashes bind reviewed sources, not an OS scope grant. Missing/mismatched profiles refuse; no guessed approved JSON or catalog snapshot is shipped as native authority.

The C fixture checks pinned sizes/offsets, performs self-only proc_pidinfo queries, retains native uint64 fields as decimal strings and reserved arrays, and captures actual fork births before readiness gates. A fixture-local fd3 record lock serializes bounded stdout frames even with a small PIPE_BUF; it is not a Run/global authority lock. The driver-native getgroups vector is retained and compared unchanged across forks, without sorting/deduplication or substituting Node's potentially augmented getgroups view. The outer parent also checks UID/EUID/GID/EGID at driver readiness.

The harness persists raw stdio and frames before opening each next gate, never replays a failed gate write, and retains actual exit separately from actual close. Raw truncation, malformed/partial frames or persistence faults cannot yield PASS_A. For a known ABI refusal it closes only the input gates and continues collecting real waits/end/close. The observation deadline writes a non-pass receipt, not a synthetic exit: an externally stopped/hung process can leave close/stdio pending for outer CI handling. There is no force-exit, unref or signal-based cleanup fallback. This limitation must remain visible when admitting the future CI job.

After actual settlements, absence is one fresh ESRCH-only sweep of positively captured fixture PIDs. The proposed8s window is an upper observation budget, not a requirement to retry: any present/denied/unknown result is retained as possible identity reuse or incomplete proof, never retried into a later green receipt. Every evidence root is retained, including successful runs. Only a complete admitted native result exits zero; all blocked/inconclusive/non-go results exit nonzero, and an unexpected failure after possible native effects is never relabeled as a pre-execution refusal.

Local checks parse the driver translation unit with Linux -fsyntax-only, exercise JS synthetic records and separately compile/execute the shared C transport core with simulated syscalls as described below. They do not link Darwin libproc, execute the C driver, establish ABI availability on macOS, or supply any first native matrix witness. Native driver/harness lifecycle and capture/cleanup behavior remain to be exercised on the exact separately admitted profile.

The R1–R4 source remediation adds an explicit-IO capture boundary, with no native fallback for unit callers. Both readable error paths mark raw capture incomplete and latch admission closed; parent descriptor release failure is recorded inside the running capture and cannot bypass its actual-close wait. A failed send is never replayed; pre-failure accepted effects remain recorded. Early acquisition cleanup attempts every acquired release and retains exact single or ordered aggregate originals; post-spawn receipts keep private exact causes and persist getter-free ordered summaries. JS capture fault injection covers stream errors, release/cleanup faults including primitive/opaque causes, short/failed writes, persistence, deadline/timer setup and gate writes, without process/filesystem hooks or real children. C transport separately retains its first errno and byte progress, emits bounded best-effort diagnostics through nonblocking stderr (including first-cause fields on secondary faults), and stops stdout after a terminal failure. The diagnostic wire/parser and raw stderr retention retain their JS synthetic checks; the C-unit below additionally executes the actual shared emitter against explicit syscall shims. Neither layer proves native availability or real Darwin descriptor/lock behavior. These fixes admit neither native execution nor a CI workflow change.

The N1 follow-up handles Node's asynchronous failed-spawn path (including EMFILE/ENFILE with null/undefined stdio). The child error listener is installed before optional stream access. A missing immediate PID remains an attempt with birth=null and close=null; no late PID reconstruction, birth/exit file or PID probe is permitted. Its Node close event is retained separately as handle_close, not a workload exit status or absence proof. Attempt persistence and deferred owned-resource cleanup follow the provider error, preserving original/cleanup ordering and single release; the capture still awaits the actual handle-close event. Native CLI consumers preserve those causes and refuse the unbound attempt before reading process-close fields or probing PIDs. Non-native regressions cover both observer modes, absent/mixed/available pipe shapes, late PID and unbound exit events, and persistence/descriptor/parent-release faults. No C emitter or Darwin runtime evidence is added by N1.

**Opt-in shared C transport unit — simulated syscalls, not PASS_A.** The [host-only C unit](../tests/fixtures/macos-spi-transport-unit.c) and driver include one transport core; no copied algorithm, whole-driver include or Darwin stub backend is used. Test-local compile-time bindings replace fcntl, write, clock_gettime, poll and getpid only inside the core. The test reporter uses ordinary libc stdout/stderr outside that boundary. Finite scripts validate arguments and retain ordered calls, errno, requested buffers and separately accepted byte prefixes; exhaustion/mismatch records failure and supplies a terminal shim error, never success. Each terminal case also refuses a later emit without additional core IO. First-cause and partial-progress state is checked independently of diagnostic delivery.

The executed 19-case cohort covers full/short writes, lock/write EINTR/EAGAIN recovery, retry-budget boundaries, terminal lock/write/zero-write faults, initial/retry clock faults, combined write/unlock failure, unlock-only failure, unavailable/EINTR/EAGAIN/EPIPE/partial stderr and format overflow. Success calls deliberately leave unrelated errno values. JS assertions decode bytes actually formed by C; complete accepted diagnostics alone count as delivered records. Failed attempts and partial prefixes are retained separately, never repaired or promoted to complete raw capture. Host errno constants are not macOS errno-number evidence.

The existing tests/installed.scripts.test.ts owner runs this only when PI_ACTORS_C_TRANSPORT_UNIT=1; ordinary runs show an explicit opt-in skip and do not invoke a compiler. The first unit host is local Linux, not a Linux-only product support decision. Unsupported host, wrong flag or missing compiler blocks/fails a requested cohort; there is no install/download fallback. PI_ACTORS_C_TRANSPORT_CC may name one existing compiler executable, never shell-split flags. The owner snapshots exact unit/header bytes into a retained /tmp/pi-actors-validation/ct-* root, compiles only that unit with C11/Wall/Wextra/Werror/O2, and records source/header/binary hashes, real parent-captured compiler/unit handles, raw stdio and actual closes through the explicit capture port. It never runs pilot driver, native probes, jobs/services or controlling signals. Successful and failed roots remain retained.

A local opt-in invocation from the repository root, not a native-pilot/CI command:

```bash
mkdir -p /tmp/pi-actors-validation
TMPDIR=/tmp/pi-actors-validation npm_config_cache=/tmp/pi-actors-validation/npm-cache PI_ACTORS_C_TRANSPORT_UNIT=1 npm exec -- node --import ./tests/test-environment.mjs --experimental-strip-types --test --test-name-pattern='^portable C transport unit' tests/installed.scripts.test.ts
```

This adds executed C logic evidence only. Actual Darwin ABI/libc/syscalls, main's real descriptor/O_NONBLOCK setup, fd3 lock inheritance/serialization across fork, real backpressure/stdio delivery and native lifecycle/absence still require the independently admitted exact-profile witness. A passing C-unit is not PASS_A, C1, B admission or backend activation; it does not authorize a workflow, native trial or later matrix row.

**Research workflow authoring checkpoint — no CI execution.** The manual-only workflow defaults both execute_pilot_a and inventory_only to false, requires exactly one selected mode before claiming any evidence root, refuses replay attempts and mismatched approved/GITHUB_SHA/GITHUB_WORKFLOW_SHA before checkout, and never cancels an in-progress trial for another dispatch. Its fresh job-owned /tmp/pi-actors-validation base is not reused, removed or uploaded if already present. prepare/collect/admit CLI entrypoints require the manual native host context and the exact run/attempt/source/workflow/repository marker created by the claiming step; they cannot adopt an existing local validation base. Reviewed JSON travels through environment data, is digest-checked as exact UTF-8 bytes, and stays outside checkout; no approved tuple is generated from job observations. The [envelope/evidence helper](../tests/fixtures/macos-spi-research.mjs) performs declared-profile/source binding only; the existing native harness remains the actual environment-tuple admission owner. If no reviewed full tuple is available, native execution stays closed; any inventory-only run needs its own admission.

**Inventory-only authoring checkpoint.** The separately selected inventory_only mode requires an approved exact source/workflow commit but no pre-existing v2 profile; both profile inputs must be empty. Their workflow fields are optional only for this mode: pilot preparation still requires independently reviewed exact JSON/digest and source binding, and the native step additionally requires execute_pilot_a=true plus inventory_only=false. Selecting both modes fails before checkout/root creation; neither selected leaves the job disabled. No metadata-to-pilot transition, profile approval or native invocation is automatic.

The inventory step declares ten fixed argv commands: git status/HEAD, sw_vers version/build, uname release/native architecture, xcodebuild -version, and xcrun SDK version/build/clang --version. A shell capture retains each invocation's raw stdout/stderr and observed shell status before proceeding; a failure stops later commands without inventing their receipts. Nonzero status may include a pre-exec/redirection failure and is not an invented native process exit. These are metadata-command observations, not process-birth/native-scope/closure proof. Node24 host metadata records full Node version, numeric UID/EUID/GID/EGID, supplementary groups, ImageVersion and the fixed job-local DEVELOPER_DIR; it never dumps the environment. No C compilation, driver, SPI, privilege change, SDK installation or service registration occurs on this path. Tool-version executables run only in a separately admitted future CI inventory, never in local authoring tests. HOME/TMPDIR/clang-cache point to inventory-scratch outside both collector source roots and the upload allowlist.

The existing collector/quotas are shared, not a second inventory exporter: mandatory inventory-request/host/prepare and stdout/stderr/code receipts precede optional workflow files. Complete inventory needs the actual inventory outcome success, actual native outcome skipped, clean matching source commit, all ten zero-code receipts, native arm64 and a complete host/tuple snapshot; malformed, missing, interrupted or failed data remains bounded non-positive salvage. The export records mode=inventory, INVENTORY_ONLY, inventory_complete and measured_tuple with separate requested_profile; it never creates requested-profile.json, a profile-v2 schema/digest or PASS_A. review_candidate/profile_approved/pilot_executed/C1/B remain false even for a complete inventory. External admission additionally checks the observed job mode, inventory/native/collector outcomes, exact job identity and complete manifest; full-write-then-error cannot admit inventory when the collector failed. A human must review measured facts and explicitly approve a later full v2 profile and separate pilot dispatch; image drift or missing metadata never repairs itself into approval.

Only contents:read is granted. Checkout does not persist credentials; Node24 setup does not run package-manager work. No npm install/test/build, SDK installation, xcode-select, privilege/service operation or release action is added. Research limits are 15min/job, 5min/native step, 2min/inventory, 1min/envelope/collection/export-admission and 3min/upload. The existing 180s capture deadline remains observation, not a global CLI kill; timeout/cancellation/VM loss supplies no process-closure proof. Automatic native replay is refused; a separately approved new dispatch is not a repair of old uncertain effects.

The collector uses an explicit filesystem IO port, freezes regular top-level workflow/native evidence bytes and records per-file hashes; it excludes only native home/tmp/clang-cache directories, not primary receipts. It saves mandatory workflow files and frozen producer inventories before required native receipts/raw files, then spends any remaining budget on optional regular files. An unavailable/malformed inventory permits bounded non-pass salvage, never a fabricated complete inventory. Only one native invocation root may form a review candidate; the root limit remains16. Excess, symlinks, ambiguous roots, partial raw stdout, missing receipts and mismatched binary/profile/source data yield no review candidate, never primary-file truncation. The owner projection is reused for captured-frame/close/absence consistency.

**Upload-budget boundary.** The complete upload allowlist is bundle/ plus the exact workflow/job-owner.txt file; raw workflow/ directory/glob fallback is removed. UB-2 aligns upload with the collector's existing regular-file inventory: include-hidden-files=true preserves already-selected dotfiles inside the bounded bundle instead of silently dropping manifest entries. This does not add another source root or recurse into excluded native home/tmp/clang-cache directories. Static workflow and memory-IO regressions cover dotfile bytes/digests/reservations, matching export eligibility and continued cache exclusion; they do not execute the upload Action. The marker is bounded before creation to1024bytes. Total exported file contents are bounded to33554432bytes/256file slots:32505856bytes/254slots for evidence, plus1047552bytes/one slot for bundle/collection.json and1024bytes/one slot for bootstrap. A bundled copy of the marker is independently charged as evidence. The4MiB per-evidence-file cap remains. Byte reservations precede writes, including growth after stat; failed or partial writes do not refund bytes or slots and are not given successful-copy digests. These are content limits, not ZIP/directory/transport-overhead or heap/work bounds. Before its sole manifest write, the collector chooses a full manifest or a bounded non-pass summary with explicit inventory/diagnostic omissions. Original causes remain private and ordered; bounded summaries are not lossless serialization of opaque originals. A metadata failure cannot rewrite an earlier manifest into green or red.

**External export eligibility (UB-1).** A positive JSON file is not a receipt of its own successful persistence: full-write-then-error may leave valid positive JSON behind. The workflow gives collection an explicit collector id, then invokes the existing helper's admit mode with the selected job mode and native/inventory/collector outcomes sourced from workflow inputs/Actions step metadata, not manifest claims. Pilot eligibility requires native/collector success; inventory-only eligibility instead requires inventory/collector success and native skipped. Both require a complete fault-free bounded manifest with coherent mode-specific file receipts and matching run/attempt/source/workflow/repository identity. A collector failure/cancellation/skipped step therefore forbids acceptance even if upload succeeds and positive JSON survives; no manifest retry or overwrite repairs it. Consumers must retain/check the external admission/step evidence rather than accept standalone review_candidate text. Eligibility still has review_required=true and never grants C1/B authority. Native outcome remains independent of collection/admission/upload failures. always() upload preserves only the positively job-owned allowlist, with14-day retention and an error on missing files. If checkout/Node/collection is unavailable, bootstrap alone is not a complete archive; actual step outcomes remain Actions facts. Hard cancellation may prevent upload entirely.

**RW-1 completeness boundary.** The native harness now owns a closed pi-actors-spi-a-evidence-v1 inventory. It writes an incomplete initial declaration, extends it synchronously before every preflight launch, binds stdout/stderr byte lengths and SHA-256 after actual close, and seals it only for a positive owner projection with every preflight settled; a write failure latches the producer closed. The final report binds exact evidence-inventory.json bytes with evidence_inventory_sha256. Inventory/persistence failure remains INCONCLUSIVE, not a retained PASS_A. This is a file-evidence protocol, not native scope authority.

Before review_candidate, the collector requires the original workflow/requested-profile.json bytes and verifies their SHA-256 against the reviewed envelope plus exact profile-field equality; the native reserialized requested-profile.json is not a byte substitute. It also requires workflow ownership/envelope/prepare/harness files, every canonical native inventory member and all declared preflight birth/exit/close/stdout/stderr files. Each preflight must match its declared command/args, captured parent/credentials and exact attempt/birth correspondence, zero-code/null-signal settlement, complete raw IO, no timeout and no capture faults. Frozen stdout/stderr lengths and digests must match the producer inventory; same-length corruption and truncation are non-pass, not merely present files. Undeclared preflight files, incomplete/noncanonical inventory, missing members or digest/receipt mismatches prevent a candidate. Available files are frozen before completeness validation; a per-file read/copy failure preserves its private original cause and continues bounded salvage without repairing the failure. Manifest persistence is attempted once, retaining ordered original/later failures. Memory-IO regressions remove every required file and cover byte/digest/receipt mismatches, the original missing-profile/preflight counterexample and opaque/undefined IO faults. No native execution is added by this checkpoint; the changed harness hash needs independent approval in any future native profile.

Actions are immutable-pinned to official repository refs verified during authoring: [checkout3d3c42e](https://github.com/actions/checkout/commit/3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1), [setup-node949feb2](https://github.com/actions/setup-node/commit/949feb2413d6458794dcd2491c4babbbce0c15c1), [upload-artifactcf430e0](https://github.com/actions/upload-artifact/commit/cf430e030ddbb5b0abf93d22962f4752f3646cd9). Official v7 refs and action.yml Node24 runtimes were inspected read-only; Actions themselves were not executed or exhaustively security-audited. No profile tuple or Action SHA placeholder is shipped. Existing installed-script tests cover the actual pure request/result guards and explicit-memory-IO collector, plus static workflow wiring; they do not run Actions, macOS inventory, driver/SPI or C binaries. Runner compatibility, actual Actions behavior and evidence retrieval remain native-CI acceptance work.

**A outcomes.** PASS_A requires a reviewable complete witness for all admitted observation cases and confirmed fixture cleanup on that exact profile. It says only that the chosen observational ABI/cases worked. NO_GO_PROFILE identifies an unavailable/denied/incompatible interface on the admitted profile, not all-macOS impossibility. ENVIRONMENT_BLOCKED identifies an unadmitted/missing execution profile; INCONCLUSIVE covers incomplete/contradictory frames, source/lifetime uncertainty or unproven settlement. Only PASS_A is a positive research result; other statuses must remain non-pass/blocked even if artifact upload succeeds. A report-only or skipped execution is not PASS_A. No numeric exit-code protocol is selected here.

**B — issuer/bootstrap and complete-scope feasibility, gated.** PASS_A permits review, not automatic transition. Before any B implementation or experiment, name one concrete issuer path, its authentic caller/grant, pre-work root/Run-generation binding, exclusive native scope identity, retained lifetime, supported OS/SDK/SPI profile, exact setup/privilege needs and rollback/cleanup boundaries. The current candidates do not supply that path: root alone cannot mint privileged-coalition membership; a launchd label, PID, unregister callback or parsed diagnostic is not a scope grant. A transient launchd job, app-bundle/ServiceManagement distribution, administrator approval, entitlement/FDA or private binding requires its own explicit admission before use. Without an admissible issuer path B stays blocked, not a prototype around assumed authority.

B's candidate must establish a generation-safe actuation primitive, sealed admission, authoritative membership/emptiness and recovery. A PID/birth check followed by a PID-only signal is not atomic lifetime-bound actuation. coalition_terminate is not the missing whole-scope Kill, and tasks-started/exited or aggregate counters alone are not an authoritative empty/closure receipt. Reject the candidate if these contracts cannot be established; do not fill the gap with polling, saved metadata or a process-group fallback.

**B falsifiers and decision.** Start with the smallest authenticated own-scope/foreign-control pair, then bounded cases for fork/setsid and controlled external-service ingress, ingress/stop contention, actual original-owner death, independent live same-session-id host, paused-but-live negative owner, natural/first-cause precedence, owner/controller loss/replacement, stale identity and partial persistence/actuation. Capture real births/readiness/parent closes, preserve the unrelated control and original failures, and require positive full scope closure before finalization/guard release. External-service work must either join the authenticated scope before work or refuse before work; no implicit external-service exception. Unknown identity, authority, membership, actuation or closure means no positive result, no destructive replay and retained evidence.

A B-success decision only permits subsequent backend design/review on the tested profile: all required scope/issuer/seal/closure and isolation properties must have real native witnesses. It is not C1/C2/C3/B2/D acceptance or permission for default/public activation. Failure of a required property rejects that candidate/configuration; unresolved evidence stays inconclusive. Do not widen privileges, change workload compatibility, drop macOS or substitute a VM to force success. The canonical BACKLOG owns admission and execution tasks; this document owns the plan and proof boundaries.

**Falsification before default activation.** Real source/npm public launches must cover owner death after admitted binding, death at each bind/spawn boundary, trusted session replacement, original-identity reuse, an independently live same-id host, authentic cross-owner children, fork/setsid escape, supervisor loss/restart and partial persistence/actuation. Include a paused/hung-but-live owner negative case so liveness uncertainty cannot become a timeout-based Kill. Capture actual births/readiness/close where observable; use fresh supported absence proof before cleanup, retain uncertain state and preserve ordered faults. Native CI results must correspond to the candidate on each agreed OS; platform-name injection and an old HEAD's green matrix are not evidence for the working tree. Bare AgentSession.dispose still needs a trusted shutdown-before-invalidation host contract.

This records the adopted direction, not completion of C1 or implementation/acceptance of owner-loss termination. BACKLOG.md alone owns the next executable slices: macOS feasibility first, then concrete native authority/bootstrap/compatibility proof within C1; live membership and positive closure remain C2/C3, with B2/D integration and native CI still required. Neither design adoption, existing graceful-quit test preparation nor a local green suite declares prerelease readiness.

### Readonly Retained-Origin Timing

Private `RunSupervisionOwner.observeTime(expected)` now explicitly samples elapsed through the exact origin retained at genuine generation admission. It first resolves the existing owner record, validates its pinned root/marker frame and fresh canonical owner/generation/normalized clock, requires the captured clock signature, then uses the existing timebase. It returns frozen identity/basis since_admission/timing plus unchanged(), or explicit missing-publication/foreign-clock/frame/owner-stopped/released/closed refusal. Outer available means the binding was observed; inner timing may still be unproven with null elapsed/remaining. Since-admission time is not verified workload liveness, active Run elapsed selection, frozen natural-terminal duration or native closure, and its basis intentionally differs from snapshot live/terminal timing.

No origin is created/backfilled, allowance renewed or policy inferred from actor values. The monotonic provider is sampled once when its shared evidence is healthy, with no later wall sample. The existing first clock fault/due facts remain sticky across observers/controllers. This is readonly with respect to files, policy, work and controllers, not a pure getter: it can advance the origin's shared monotonic/fault observations. It neither claims nor starts a controller, schedules/cancels a wake, notifies due, signals or ACKs. Another owner may keep the controller claim while this owner observes time. Existing snapshot() remains a distinct no-IO/no-time-sampling projection.

Retention identity/logical stop/close and metadata frames are rechecked before and after sampling; reentrant stop/release/close or generation/clock changes discard output rather than returning a stale elapsed value or resurrecting a record. Returned unchanged() rechecks retention and captured canonical/frame observations without sampling the provider again, not future liveness, refreshed time, global storage immutability or a multi-file transaction. Role bodies, result/stop/Trace evidence, directories for discovery and process APIs are not read/probed; only canonical ownership/clock metadata uses its existing bounded strict readers. The port reads no lifecycle, so even an existing terminal result cannot make a later elapsed sample into a frozen terminal fact.

Five owning properties cover enabled/disabled policy, unchanged-sample snapshots, no controller claim/timer/notify including another holder, missing/foreign/copied frames before sampling, sticky reversal/epoch/provider faults with preserved due, reentrant revocation/canonical change, and readonly/no-body/no-wall/no-process behavior. The real fresh-launch test additionally refuses this port during unpublished admission, accepts the retained since-admission observation after publication and still leaves the owner retained before explicit start. These are bounded local timing/retention witnesses, not independent supervisor lifetime, cross-process origin recovery, positive native enforcement or public snapshot acceptance. Ordinary detached-runner frozen timing, default coordinator lifetime and public acceptance remain open. In the ordinary launch, the original monotonic origin remains in the caller process while the natural-result producer is admitted inside the detached runner; the current handoff supplies no original-origin/first-natural-acceptance bridge. Healthy saved clocks/results, the diagnostics-only candidate token or a newly captured worker clock cannot fill that gap. The natural-only commit admission below distinguishes successful natural publication from pending captures and failed publication, but does not supply the original origin or a frozen sample. That remaining join is not supplied by a late readonly observation.

### Private First-Natural Timing Admission

`publishRunResult` optionally calls kernel-only `RunResultPublicationOptions.naturalResultTiming` synchronously after its first successful natural root-result write and before Trace. No observer means no new token or retention. Duplicate results, earlier source/prepare failure, invalid authority and post-stop captures never emit this event. A root replacement that physically occurred before an I/O error is still preserved, but a failed publication call does not backfill an observer from the now-readable file. This is neither rollback nor proof that no bytes committed.

The original opaque `RunNaturalResultTimingAdmission` works only in its same-module/runtime callback. `withRunNaturalResultTimingAdmission` requires the exact producer/root/token and freshly revalidates producer authority around a synchronous action under the already-held lifecycle lock. It exposes only frozen identity/code/recorded status/completed_at with natural acceptance and timing not_asserted, never origin, elapsed, diagnostics, values, private paths or body permission. Copies, serialization, prototypes, a diagnostic token and returned timing-shaped DTOs cannot create that admission. No model/work callback is awaited, no clock sampled and no controller, signal or public parameter enabled.

`readRunNaturalResultTimingAcceptance` is deliberately a pure passive historical receipt. It records successful kernel publication, not current file readability/status, immutable storage, native closure or lock release. Receipt data survives source loss and cannot reopen the expired active callback. The completed_at string is descriptive wall metadata, never a duration or permission to subtract wall timestamps. The separate retained-origin consumer below joins a sample captured in the active first-natural event with its own lifetime and freshly admitted canonical lifecycle; the passive receipt alone cannot authorize a new late sample or a healthy snapshot.

Active use is revoked on all observer/action returns and throws. Deferred/then-protocol returns refuse without custom then/catch execution; original errors and partial effects survive. Observer failure happens after acceptance, so the natural result/code remains committed and is never retried into a synthetic workload failure. Later Trace failure likewise cannot erase the recorded acceptance. Five owning properties cover original/copy/cross-scope admission, natural-versus-capture/duplicate/earlier failure, before/after-replacement I/O and Trace errors, callback/action refusal, and pure historical reads with terminal timing still unproven. These are isolated same-runtime producer fixtures, not a caller-to-worker origin bridge, public/default timing or native-scope acceptance.

### Private Original-Origin First-Natural Freeze

`RunSupervisionOwner.captureNaturalTime(expected, producer, admission, stateDir?)` is a separate synchronous kernel consumer. It requires this owner's original retained origin/root/identity/frame and the exact active postcommit first-natural token and producer. It samples through the existing original observeTime method under the already-held publication admission, with clock/lifetime/frame and producer fences around the sample. It never subtracts wall timestamps, reconstructs an origin or accepts a serialized timing candidate. No ordinary caller or detached runner is wired to it.

The first authorized attempt is final for participating owners sharing that exact original origin in this module/runtime. A provisional capturing slot prevents reentrant sampling; healthy elapsed is frozen once only after the action's producer and retained-source fences succeed. Failed clock/frame/lifetime proof stays unproven rather than being retried or repaired from a later healthy observation. Invalid copied/foreign/expired admission fails before consuming a sampling slot. A second call in the same original active event reuses the frozen fact without sampling; an expired token still cannot invoke capture. Same-origin logical handover preserves the frozen fact and original allowance, not a cross-process lease or durable recovery record.

`observeFrozenNaturalTime` consumes the original frozen fact with fresh owned clock/frame and local lifetime admission. Its first_natural_acceptance basis carries exact elapsed plus historical code/status/completed_at, not current lifecycle or native closure. It reads no current clock, process, result/role body, source/target body or directory discovery and performs no write, scheduling, controller claim/start, notification, signal or ACK. Later clock-provider faults cannot rewrite an earlier proven sample. Stop/release/close revoke this owner's consumption. The reading's unchanged fence observes its retained clock/frame/lifetime without resampling, not future storage immutability or source health.

A fifth optional kernel port on readRunSnapshotFacts must be that original retained-owner method, not actor JSON, a cached DTO or a fabricated callback. The existing single parsed canonical natural result must match the frozen identity/code/status/completed_at exactly before elapsed becomes terminal. Literal-true synchronous timing and file/owner fences remain required. Missing capture keeps timing_unproven; malformed or changed acceptance refuses the join. The three-argument metadata-only default still never supplies duration. The owner compositor supplies both original timing ports and captured privacy references, so same-runtime genuine natural0/7 can now yield private done/failed snapshots and literal text/JSON without fresh time/native probes.

Eight owning properties cover one sample across participating owners, logical handover, natural0/7 rendering, copied/foreign/late inputs and missed events, sticky failed attempts, recursive/revoked/replaced-frame sampling, readonly frozen reads and later clock faults, single-result canonical joining/mismatched acceptance, deferred/accessor/fence refusal, separate result/sample/observer/Trace failures and owner/generation/clock changes. They use genuine same-runtime result producers with controlled original clocks, not the ordinary caller-origin/detached-worker bridge or real SDK frozen-time acceptance. Ordinary bridge/lifetimes, public/default/manual/timed routes, visual acceptance and native enforcement remain gated. Accepted result, frozen observation and Trace are separate effects: later observer/Trace failure does not roll back accepted work or authorize a replacement sample.

Separate source/packed acceptance now executes four finite native commands through the real command executor and genuine same-runtime kernel producers, natural0/7 per layout. An actual monotonic provider retains T0 before work and samples T1 in the active postcommit callback; the two values bracket real work and their exact floored difference equals the frozen snapshot. Copied/late token calls and duplicate publication cannot resample. Original privacy references survive later metadata/cwd/secret changes; delayed terminal reads preserve elapsed, literal text/JSON and body exclusion, while default facts stay timing_unproven. Controller/deadline ports remain unused. This is privileged isolated metadata/reference/owner composition under the real lifecycle lock, not ordinary startRun, SDK reporting or a caller-to-worker bridge. Four producer hosts and four workload PIDs are captured before subsequent assertions/awaits. Native-close codes match actual commands; workload fresh ESRCH is recorded before completion, but the producer manifest explicitly leaves its own exit not_asserted. Only the parent's fresh ESRCH checks for both roles immediately before deletion admit cleanup, retaining exact primary/manifest/probe failures on uncertainty. No native membership/exhaustive closure, cross-process origin authority or public/visual acceptance follows.

### Caller/Worker Clock Boundary: Investigation, Not a Carrier

Source inspection establishes three separate boundaries. The default source in run-supervision.ts uses process-relative performance.now with a module-local object epoch and private WeakMap start/provider evidence; the origin's JSON contains only wall_ms. With the opt-in kernel handoff enabled, async-runs.ts retains that original caller object before spawning; the default path has no such handoff, while the ordinary detached runner has only ignore/stdout/stderr descriptors, no admitted IPC clock carrier, and prefers its compiled runtime modules. scripts/async-runner.mjs admits the result producer in its own process and does not wire naturalResultTiming. Both producer and natural-event authority are runtime-local: copying data or importing the same filename in another process cannot recreate their private maps.

A bounded disposable Linux/Node26 inquiry explicitly added an experimental IPC channel to one scratch-state producer; this was not an ordinary startRun/SDK route or a workload-enforcement witness. Its two process-relative performance clocks had distinct time origins: directly mixing caller T0 and child event values yielded about0.78ms, while the observed hrtime counter difference was about321.51ms. The caller deliberately held its event loop across the child event; its later original-origin observation read462ms, with about140.37ms observed channel delay. The child's original active natural admission worked inside its callback and was revoked before the caller handled the message. The child rejected the copied caller origin, and the caller rejected the copied timing token; the child's passive receipt remained historical timing not_asserted. The synchronization file supplied no lifecycle or time authority. One captured producer PID/frame was freshly ESRCH-probed immediately before scratch deletion; no signal or native policy change occurred.

These measurements falsify a direct mixed-performance subtraction and the assumption that parent notification/late sampling equals first-natural timing. They do not authenticate an OS-global clock domain or prove portable hrtime/boot/namespace/VM continuity. Installed Node type comments describe hrtime as relative to an arbitrary past and intended for interval measurement; they are not a cross-process epoch/issuer guarantee. performance.timeOrigin is an epoch timestamp, not this kernel's shared epoch/issuer capability; combining it with relative samples would still require separately admitted clock-domain/continuity proof. completedAt wall subtraction never supplies retained monotonic authority. An asynchronous RPC cannot keep the current synchronous callback/held lifecycle admission open without changing its contract; a sync wait for a busy caller introduces blocking/deadlock/controller-lifetime obligations, not a free bridge.

No production carrier is selected by this inquiry. A future live launcher-bound carrier needs an explicitly supported monotonic domain/epoch, the unchanged original T0, a genuine privileged issuer/bootstrap binding to exact root/generation/producer, retained/revocable lifetime, and a worker-local first-natural sampling point that does not await a model or caller under the lifecycle lock. Numerical correspondence, an inherited descriptor, a nonce, a saved clock or a decoded packet alone establishes none of that authority. Replacing the original T0 with a worker-current anchor would omit startup and renew allowance; the existing start mismatch must not be hidden by rewriting metadata. Moving canonical admission into another process instead would be an explicit lifecycle/clock-contract redesign, not a drop-in repair. Existing private same-runtime acceptance stays valid; ordinary bridge adoption still needs a reviewed approved contract and platform/authority evidence, without silently crossing C's credential/platform gate or claiming native membership/closure.

### Draft: Live Original-Clock Carrier — Not Adopted

**Status and scope.** This is a reviewable admission/proof proposal, not an accepted API, transport, clock backend or platform policy. No carrier exists in the implementation, and these conceptual roles authorize no new port, public input or default activation. The proposed first slice would be optional fresh-generation observational timing only, preserving the existing admission T0, startup/retry/nested-work allowance and natural0/7 truth. Strict enforcement, independent supervisor lifetime, restart recovery, work/native membership, body visibility and scope closure remain separately gated. Adoption requires explicit contract approval plus concrete domain/bootstrap/platform proof; the Linux inquiry above supplies neither.

**Recommendation for review.** Prefer a live kernel-origin delegation with worker-local T1 over parent-notification sampling, because only the former could preserve the accepted temporal boundary without a synchronous RPC. This is a direction to evaluate, not a claim that an inherited descriptor or Node hrtime implements it. Moving canonical admission into another process is a different lifecycle proposal requiring an explicit T0/launch-receipt contract change. Replacing T0 after worker startup, wall alignment and replaying saved packets are not candidate repairs.

**Ownership, not a new registry.** The existing clock owner would own physical domain/epoch evidence, original anchor capture, units/precision and sticky faults. The existing launch/bootstrap owners would own original issuer/receiver lifetime and exact fresh launch binding. The existing result owner would own local first-natural admission and publication/cleanup boundaries. The retained supervision owner would own accepting a previously frozen remote fact, local lifetime, canonical joins and captured reference privacy. No new module, wire format, registry or exported function name is selected here; earn any later boundary through actual implementation/dependency pressure, not these conceptual roles.

**Proposed flow and refusal contract:**

1. **Capture, never reanchor.** A clock provider with demonstrated shared-domain/epoch semantics must already be admitted at the existing fresh-generation T0. Derive any later export from that original retained anchor and frozen policy, not a new worker/caller sample or a later calibration against another clock. Old/default performance origins, saved metadata, singleton reuse and restarted runtimes cannot be upgraded by copying data. Exact monotonic correspondence, nonnegative safe-integer elapsed, floor/ceil rules and uncertainty must be preserved; an unproved conversion or rounding tolerance supplies no healthy duration. Channel creation/handoff must still follow complete fallible metadata preparation; a new carrier cannot reopen the capture/native/resource leaks guarded by launch preparation.
2. **Bind a live launch, not a packet.** A privileged original launcher must retain the exact root/marker frame, owner/generation/clock signature and one fresh launch attempt. Bootstrap must establish a genuine receiver and exact producer/birth correspondence before it can mint a receiver-local delegated clock capability. Actor arguments, environment values, marker shape, PID, nonce, descriptor, hash or decoded bytes alone do not authenticate that path. Unsupported identity/domain or ambiguous ingress refuses timing delegation. Payload receipt may occur outside lifecycle admission, but final local admission must synchronously revalidate the already-held fresh producer/frame. Do not await a child acknowledgement while holding the caller start lock or await RPC/model/work inside producer admission. A failed optional observation bootstrap must not fabricate a workload exit; any future stricter pre-work refusal policy requires separate approval.
3. **Sample locally at first-natural.** Only the genuine receiver-local original-anchor capability plus the existing original producer and active postcommit first-natural admission may select T1. The provider read must be synchronous and local to the sampling runtime, not a call to a busy caller. Recheck exact root/generation/owner/clock/domain/lifetime and producer around the sample. Preserve the first authorized attempt, including failed/unproven capture, without duplicate/reconnect/restart repair. Post-stop captures, failed result writes, earlier source failure and unavailable clock proof yield no terminal timing. Metadata or serialized diagnostic/natural tokens cannot substitute for either capability.
4. **Separate capture and dispatch.** Root acceptance, frozen time, Trace, lifecycle cleanup and transport are distinct effects. Later failures preserve the real accepted result/code and any already admitted historical frozen fact; no rollback, synthetic failure or replacement sample follows. Capture may only retain or synchronously enqueue a bounded immutable kernel return record, never await transport/model/work completion or invoke delivery callbacks. Later relay of an already admitted historical fact grants no new producer operation and must not imply that publication return/absence of a cleanup diagnostic proves lock release. Cleanup quarantine continues to fence fresh producer operations without erasing accepted facts. If a chosen transport requires a released lock, its adapter must establish that positive boundary or refuse; do not invent a generic post-lock capability for transports that do not need one. Concrete nonblocking relay/cleanup behavior remains a required reviewed mechanism, not an implicit retry or ACK.
5. **Admit a frozen return, not remote sampling permission.** A delayed return may carry only an already captured identity/code/status/completed_at/elapsed fact and its kernel-owned source binding, never body/private paths or an active token for the caller to replay. The original caller receiver must authenticate the live launch/delegation and sampling issuer, bind the fact to the retained original T0/domain/epoch and that exact authorized sampling point, admit its current local epoch/root/clock and match one fresh canonical natural result before retaining the fact. These are new proof obligations, not a decoder authenticating JSON. The current captureNaturalTime API cannot consume that remote packet. Losing or restoring a file, copied receipt, actor field or new same-id session cannot recreate return admission. Later observation consumes retained frozen evidence with existing source/lifetime fences and original captured cwd/index privacy; it never samples a late T1 or refreshes privacy.
6. **Name lifetime boundaries.** Before delegation admission, cancelled launch, replaced frame, stale issuer or lost/ambiguous bootstrap means no imported capability. After a fully admitted delegation, caller busy/idle or channel EOF cannot silently renew T0, stop work, erase a proven fact or imply global revocation: the worker's already delegated clock lifetime is distinct from the caller's local view lifetime. Worker sampling still requires its own original retained domain/producer/generation to remain valid. Caller replacement/disposal rejects late return consumption; no copied id or unknown invalidated wrapper adopts the old lease. Worker/provider/domain loss stays unproven and cannot be recovered from serialized state. No cross-process controller exclusivity, native closure, hung-worker independence or durable takeover is promised by this timing delegation.

**Proof obligations before adoption:**

- **Domain/provider:** Establish common counter scale/precision and the exact meaning of epoch for each explicitly agreed platform, including discontinuity/suspend/namespace/VM/provider-loss behavior or explicit bounded refusal. A one-host hrtime ordering witness, timeOrigin timestamp or unit fake proves no portable domain. Do not silently select Linux-only support, change credentials or adopt C's native mechanism to make a clock test pass.
- **Issuer/bootstrap/return:** Name the concrete trusted ingress and threat boundary, then reject copied packets/prototypes, actor-controlled environment/flags/paths, forged endpoint/nonce, wrong producer/owner/generation/root/clock, copied markers, duplicate imports, late/replayed bootstrap and same-id foreign managers. Demonstrate the original binding rather than a self-authenticating schema. This is not an arbitrary-JavaScript or same-UID security sandbox claim.
- **Temporal and synchronous:** Bracket actual finite work with the exact original T0 and worker-local natural T1, including startup/metadata/transport delay without wall subtraction. Exercise busy/unresponsive caller, return backpressure/loss and worker failure without waiting under a lifecycle lock. A stopped caller view must not authorize late sampling or resurrect retention.
- **First winner and separate effects:** Cover natural0/7, post-stop capture, duplicates, before/after-physical-replacement I/O failure, clock faults, reentrant revocation, Trace failure and cleanup quarantine. Demonstrate bounded nonblocking relay and cleanup behavior; any dependency on released locks needs real positive evidence, not an absent failure flag. Keep exact errors and partial/orphan effects, with no source repair, retry into authority or workload-code substitution.
- **Lifetime and composition:** Test loss before versus after admitted delegation, startup replacement races, source/compiled module instances, worker restart, session shutdown-before-invalidation, channel reconnect and conflicting original holders. Same-runtime cooperative claims do not prove global controller ownership. Unsupported historical generations keep timing_unproven.
- **Views and acceptance:** Require genuine source/packed journeys for bootstrap, frozen return and original-reference snapshot composition, not fixtures that directly mint a local capability or merely decode a marker. Retain complete bounds/privacy, disabled-reporting/no-wake behavior and shared literal budgets. Public/manual/timed activation, actual audit, visual acceptance and native enforcement still require their existing combined gates.

**Decision boundary.** Review must approve the delegation/worker-T1/frozen-return authority model before a prototype changes kernel interfaces. A concrete clock-domain/epoch provider, supported-platform decision, bootstrap/return issuer and bounded nonblocking relay/cleanup mechanism remain unselected; these are blocking proof obligations, not implementation placeholders that may be filled with raw hrtime/FD/nonce checks. No prototype, compatibility change or production activation is authorized by this draft. Independent A2 work may proceed without pretending this bridge or C is complete.

## Shared Run Snapshot

Manual Run inspection and timed diagnostics use one validated producer, payload schema, compact renderer, and output budget, not parallel status implementations.

The private [`projectRunSnapshot`](../lib/runs-snapshot.ts) is the initial data-only projection, not that complete producer or a public inspect route. It accepts already-admitted owner/generation, active or natural lifecycle, live or frozen-terminal elapsed facts, and curated authorized references; it does not read/admit files, measure clocks, redact/authorize paths, export evidence or acquire native authority. Unknown timing/lifecycle fails boundedly, mismatched/unavailable index facts yield `evidence: null`, and oversized output fails rather than clipping metadata or paths. Forced-stop states require later authoritative scope facts; raw legacy status is not an admitted lifecycle fact. The private owner compositor below joins active/frozen-time/reference admission; ordinary caller/worker timing continuity, default coordinator lifetime and public routing remain open.

Default `readRunSnapshotFacts(stateDir, expected, capturedAt)` in `runs-status.ts` supplies a narrow readonly canonical-fact join, not a second legacy status stack or public route. It admits exact owned generation/optional owner through `readRunContextIdentity`, bounded strictUTF8 canonical metadata/result under the independent1-MiB source allowance, and the shared context-index reader. One parsed regular-file natural outcome through the existing classifier yields done/failed only with the cooperating outcome protocol; legacy/absent/malformed/foreign/post-stop-captured/active observations stay lifecycle unproven even with live-looking PID, status/stopped/progress flags or wall timestamps. Accepted natural evidence does not require reading later stop/Trace evidence. No forced-stop/native-closure inference is added, and existing public getRunStatus compatibility is unchanged.

The caller supplies a canonical observation timestamp; the reader samples no current clock or process and ignores stored elapsed/age fields. Timing is always unproven in this default metadata-only join, including natural terminals: projectRunSnapshot consequently returns timing_unproven for accepted natural facts and lifecycle_unproven otherwise, never a fabricated complete card. This default opts into neither original timing port; ordinary runtime timing continuity still needs B1. CWD retains its captured operational spelling without target probes/normalization; valid index metadata supplies only its exact identity/path, while unavailable or malformed index omits that reference without erasing lifecycle. These cwd/index values are kernel-local metadata, not a privacy certificate, public visibility grant or role-body permission; compose the original captured-privacy reference owner before routing them to an operator/model.

The frozen fact shape excludes raw result/values/progress/tokens, uses the shared16-KiB encoded ceiling and rejects overflow rather than clipping paths. Default reads perform no writes, locks, exports, scans, artifact/session/role-body reads, process probes, scheduling or ACK. Shared index admission may open a regular role descriptor to check metadata/readability, never role bytes. Returned unchanged() fences owner/marker/canonical metadata/result/index file observations, not every future role change, a transaction or exhaustive race immunity. Following a role must freshly use its own reader. **Shared pure snapshot renderer.** `renderRunSnapshot(value, format = "text")` in `tools-response.ts` now formats already-admitted payloads, without reading evidence, role/target bodies, clocks, processes, owner change callbacks or lifecycle/delivery state. The text direction is status-first: Run, literal state/optional stopping phase and exact elapsed ms, followed by complete generation, canonical captured_at, launch cwd and index reference. Explicit JSON format emits compact lossless JSON of the same curated fields, with no extra logs/body/detail map; every payload within the existing16-KiB JSON bound fits that format without a separate pretty-print allowance. Text output has its own complete UTF-8 encoded16-KiB row ceiling under the same shared delivery budget. Fence amplification or oversized data refuses the whole view rather than clipping tracked identities/paths or silently selecting another format. An explicit JSON request can recover a valid payload whose literal text fencing would overflow; there is no implicit fallback. Invalid scalar/control data refuses rather than normalizing it into a different identity/reference; ordinary CRLF/tab whitespace in generation, BOM, genuine U+FFFD, Unicode and backticks remain literal. Tool leading spacing, transport envelopes and combined packets still need their own shared-budget accounting, never an extra allowance.

Five properties cover status-first text/JSON parity, literal field boundaries/Unicode, explicit unavailable index and invalid shape/refusal, no getters/serialization/IO/process/time effects or input mutation, and exact UTF-8 limits including text-fence amplification and explicit JSON recovery. Synthetic done/failed fixtures exercise formatting only, not frozen-terminal timing acceptance.
