The runtime/export owner prepares authorized views and atomically replaces each file and the bounded index independently of inspection; publish a reference only after its export is readable. This is not a cross-file atomic snapshot: mutable live views can advance between index and file reads, and historical excerpts still require stable captures. A missing view is marked unavailable/pending/not-applicable, not fabricated as an empty file. Before following a role, verify the index and evidence generation against the snapshot; replacement/pruning is unavailable evidence, never permission to inspect a newer generation. Context means retained authorized evidence, not a promise to reconstruct discarded history. Manual reads select a role and a bounded range using its format/size/coverage metadata; they do not require `inspect` to return the value or load every role.

## Latest-Only Delivery and Context

Use the existing root-coordinator boundary/idle scheduling and acceptance authority, not a stream of native steering messages. Periodic runtime diagnostics are a distinct typed delivery class; they do not synthesize actor-authored `attention: "steer"`, promote generic Trace, or alter urgent semantic checkpoints.

- Keep at most one latest unadmitted diagnostic per exact generation. A due snapshot is refreshed from current evidence immediately before admission.
- Freeze admitted packets and their identities. Newer observations wait separately; never mutate a packet whose Pi acceptance is pending or assume that returning a boundary draft accepted it.
- Coalesce eligible generations into one root-owned update, following existing owner/lineage and stale-context rules and the shared one-minute routine-diagnostic cooldown. Descendant Pi sessions and staggered Run timers do not gain separate wakeup or frequency allowances.
- Active updates wait until a completed safe boundary after the full tool batch. Already-idle delivery may request one debounced model wakeup for an explicitly configured due diagnostic batch; it must not request one turn per Run.
- Share the existing centralized model-output budget with completion delivery. Terminal outcomes take priority; diagnostics use stable fair ordering, disclose overflow, and retain only the latest deferred snapshot. Do not create a second unbounded journal or a separate full allowance per Run.
- In model-context projection, retain only the newest accepted diagnostic for each generation and remove obsolete diagnostic payloads after its terminal result. Do not rewrite native Pi entries, Trace, ordinary messages, foreign metadata, or accepted terminal outcomes.
- A mixed packet must preserve terminal members when older diagnostics are folded. Diagnostic replacement cannot acknowledge, erase, or redeliver a completion; completion acceptance remains generation-fenced and immutable.
- A terminal/replacement transition invalidates queued old diagnostics. Revalidate at delivery/context projection so a delayed `running` row cannot appear after that generation's outcome. Recovery follows existing proof-of-presence/absence rules and cannot replay already accepted snapshots.

Snapshot production, packet acceptance, and model projection are separate responsibilities. Bounded Trace may retain operational history, but a history of every heartbeat is neither required model input nor an audit-log promise.

## Generation-Fenced Manual Kill

Extend the public `message` envelope with top-level `run_instance_id`. It is required for `action: "kill"`; the launch response, compact inspection, or diagnostic supplies it:

```json
{
  "target": "run:review",
  "action": "kill",
  "run_instance_id": "<generation observed by the caller>"
}
```

The field is a nonempty opaque string and an exact precondition, not part of the logical Run id and not actor-local `input`. Other Run actions may supply it and must honor it; `target: "runtime"` rejects it. This increment requires it only for `kill` and does not invent a new actor-local action or consume Control capacity.

A missing kill generation fails as `run_generation_required`; a replacement generation fails as `run_generation_mismatch`. Both reject before signaling or Control admission. Never read the newest generation and silently substitute it for the caller's stale expectation. These are planned bounded machine-readable error codes, not assertions about current tool errors.

Inside the lifecycle lock, check the current coordinator owner, expected generation, process identity, and terminal state again. An exact already-terminal generation with retained authoritative proof returns an idempotent `already_terminal` acknowledgement without signals or another terminal event. If its logical id has been reused, an old request still fails generation matching rather than applying to the replacement.

A winning manual stop claim produces terminal status `killed` with reason `manual_kill`, distinct from `timed_out` with reason `max_runtime`. A pending stop request is nonterminal (`status: "running"`, `phase: "stopping"`, `stopped: false`), not a successful-kill claim. A synchronously confirmed terminal acknowledgement follows the existing stop-handled policy; a request-only acknowledgement cannot suppress the eventual automatic outcome. Concurrent manual/deadline claims serialize; the first accepted stop reason wins, with one final outcome.

## Self-Contained Automatic Outcomes

Normal finite roots deliver their outcome through the existing active/idle completion path without an `inspect` round trip. Services need not terminate to stay usable; periodic diagnostics are optional, and normal service Control semantics remain unchanged. Existing silent policies, exact root ownership, and containing-tree eligibility remain authoritative.

Keep generation, terminal status, compact summary, and declared artifacts. Add the distinct `timed_out` reason/deadline evidence and label output from interrupted work `partial: true`. Display clipping is not task partiality. Compact public-spawn and asynchronous registered-tool launch receipts now omit the three generic `inspect` calls while preserving launch facts. Explicit verbose public spawn retains existing full JSON metadata, including expert `next_actions`; meaningful diagnosis may still recommend the appropriate public inspection route. Quiet compact text adds no unconditional completion/readiness promise and does not change silent or service policy.

### Private Kernel Contract: Primary Command Text Publication

Status: Initial kernel publication/result binding and readonly saved-source admission are implemented for a bare ordinary command, one successful attempt and complete captured stdout, either equal to the complete memory result or bound to its privately observed stream digest when memory is only a tail. Initial immutable completion transport and literal operator/model projections are also implemented. The linked real public-spawn no-polling journey is exercised in source and packed SDK layouts for that initial case. This is bounded initial-case acceptance, not a public evidence-role/range API, broader selection or complete UX/AX release acceptance. The contract adds no capability registry, public permission flag, native authority or release shortcut.

#### Initial Scope and Owners

The first supported case is one ordinary command, one attempt, successful completion and its complete valid UTF-8 stdout selected as primary text before formatting. Do not implicitly extend this permission to parallel/merged output, retry selection, failure stderr, review markers or assistant-session streams.

- `execution.ts` owns primary selection in the successful `executeRegisteredTool()` branch before `formatOutput()`, not Run ownership or storage publication.
- The async runner supplies a narrow invocation-local kernel port retaining the genuine admitted producer, captured Run metadata and the binding between its `observedExec` result and the exact command/attempt. Keep the producer out of generic executor arguments and model-facing details; the port must not accept arbitrary text or paths unrelated to that binding.
- The existing context-export owner applies the fixed privacy projection and publishes the retained source. `runs-results.ts` supplies producer revalidation and lifecycle serialization; no new library domain or global capability registry is required.

#### Executor-Selected Final Response

The operator separately adopted result-first completion content: a completion reports the work's outcome, not its launch command. The complete raw-stdout contract above is unchanged. A distinct kernel-only final-response port now selects the successful executor's actual returned stdout before tool-display clipping, including composition/retry results and direct Pi text-print answers. It does not read arbitrary session histories, raw stderr, other attempts or mutable logs. Direct Pi JSON/RPC/interactive output is withheld. The runner supplies the original producer; the source owner applies its captured privacy and unchanged4MiB input bound through the existing exclusive immutable publication path.

An exported response carries text_basis:actor_response and upstream_truncated. Its exact counts cover the retained projected response, not necessarily the original complete stdout/Actor answer. Observed upstream clipping remains a separate unknown-count warning; reported killed flags on a successful degraded result do not become native closure or prohibit its response text. Failed roots gain no successful-response publication. This path cannot replace complete output, repair privacy/capture/source refusal, renew ownership or replay selection. Canonical/result/source/packet effects remain separate. New result-first captures do not expose command excerpts; all old captured layouts replay exactly.

Fresh presentation additionally follows the adopted [shared result/preview budget](coordinator-delivery.md#result-first-completion-content):48KiB including service data, equal candidate shares and a frozen prefix allocation. The source owner selects up to48KiB from the same original admitted read; publisher binding previews remain160code points and the source input remains4MiB. result denotes a whole admitted text fitting its share without observed upstream clipping, not universal Actor output or native closure; preview carries exact local loss and separate unknown upstream loss. Old headings_outcomes and earlier packets retain their original16KiB/text/hints, without reallocation or source rereads. The following earlier result-record evidence retains its historical budgets and encodings; the delivery contract owns current display behavior.

Local source/npm SDK witnesses exercise genuine Pi print replies, original retry/sequence/parallel/degraded output and byte-overflow recovery. This admits the bounded final response only, not the broader complete-raw-stream proposal below, public selectors, default streams/supervision, native truth, loaded UI or release readiness.

#### A3 Owner Reconciliation and Unadopted Selection Proposal

The A3 source inspection separates implemented retention from new publication permission. `execution.ts` selects only a successful bare string template; even a single wrapped leaf currently does not call the primary port. `createRunPrimaryTextPublisher` in `runs-context-exports.ts` independently requires one observed non-session command and exactly attempt1, binding its complete capture before privacy/export. The separate final-response path above may capture the actual retained response of a successful composition/retry/Pi text-print execution; it does not make that response a complete raw stream. Unsupported selections and failures remain explicit. Neither actor-supplied fields, formatted display nor canonical metadata can bypass the respective selection owner.

Existing acceptance already covers complete authorized primary input through4MiB, including digest-bound memory tails, omitted-primary recovery, full fixed diagnostic metadata fields and complete1800-declaration metadata recovery beside large/binary outputs. Those witnesses do not need another implementation to begin A3. Complete diagnostic `error` means the recorded field, potentially already formatted/clipped upstream, not permission for complete native stderr. Genuine retry/composition/failure/Actor-session research proves readable evidence and natural codes separately from primary eligibility.

`observability.ts` currently synthesizes ordinary terminal summaries such as `Run completed.`; it does not preserve a substantive answer as a summary source. Its member collector caps supplied summaries at1000 UTF-16 code units, and `run-delivery.ts` normalizes/caps the displayed summary at200; these collector/display cuts can still split a surrogate pair. The independent `runs-trace.ts` source-side repair now keeps the longest prefix within the unchanged1000 UTF-16-unit allowance without splitting an original surrogate pair at that boundary. It applies only to new event input, retains no additional raw text and adds no fields, normalization or original-length/clipping metadata. Previously ill-formed input is not retrospectively repaired. Reader/append/compaction preserve retained legacy event bytes, including old split-pair and longer summaries; admitted packets and their renderers are unchanged.

Downstream exporters still cannot reconstruct discarded text or certify original counts. Captured-member overflow inventories deliberately exclude summaries/legacy bodies. Richer summary/original Trace retention needs its own admitted source/privacy and compatibility decision, not a longer slice, a mutable-journal reference or retrospective full-source claim. Existing packets must retain their admitted text; a formatter correction must not silently retrofit them. Mirrored Trace owner tests verify boundary classes with an independent scalar-iteration oracle, exact returned/read/serialized text, unchanged optional fields and legacy-byte compatibility through actual compaction. The existing installed-dist boundary test also exercises the repaired writer/reader; these are source/compiled persistence checks, not new summary-source publication or rendered phone/TUI acceptance.

**Unadopted minimal next selection proposal — owner approval required.** Permit the final successful settled attempt of exactly one ordinary non-session string command, including a single retry wrapper, as the primary stdout source. Require contiguous original attempt receipts starting at1, earlier attempts unsuccessful, the exact executor-selected final result, code0/not killed and complete capture/digest binding to that attempt's own stdout file. Export only that final attempt through the existing captured privacy,4MiB input allowance and immutable source/result/packet paths; never concatenate attempts. This proposal grants no additional complete-raw-stream permission for arrays, parallel/repeat/recovery/acceptance-policy output, failure stderr or Actor-session streams; the separately adopted bounded final-response contract above is not such permission. Stop captures remain captures, not terminal/closure proof. This proposal authorizes no implementation until accepted; broader selection, richer summaries and any source-budget increase remain separate decisions.

The earlier owner reconciliation used existing executor-selection and diagnostic-source tests without changing executable inputs. The subsequent Trace scalar repair is separately validated on its retained source/compiled candidate. Neither admits retry publication, richer summary recovery or integrated release acceptance.

##### Legacy Completion Disclosure Boundary

The tracked excerpt aspect `excerpt_format: bracketed_omissions_v1` applies only to admitted primary/diagnostic counters; it neither versions legacy summary/body rendering nor certifies their original privacy. Extending that already-saved aspect to legacy text would change admitted packets. A separate explicitly admitted new-capture format is required before changing those fields, with the A3 privacy/retained-stage and packet-compatibility decision kept explicit; the approved bracketed display rule is not broader source retention or permission to reconstruct counts.

The existing owners have distinct loss stages:

- **Production summary:** observability synthesizes short status text, not an authored answer. Its collector additionally caps externally supplied summaries at1000 UTF-16 units; delivery normalizes whitespace/backticks and caps the displayed summary at200. The old owner reconciliation already identifies these cuts and the separate source/compatibility gate; this preparation does not repeat it as new behavior.
- **Legacy stdout:** observability reads at most8192bytes, decodes UTF8 non-strictly, trims, and then clips at4000 UTF-16 units before removing an optional review marker. Collector body handling caps at4000 again. Raw captured lengths and a private upstream tail are not admitted exact-count/privacy evidence. A short retained body or a missing ellipsis does not establish complete input.
- **Packet/renderer:** the closed member schema retains only bounded summary/output strings, without a legacy loss flag, original range/count or source-privacy binding. Captured overflow inventories deliberately omit both. Packet formatting/phases reload saved values without consulting stdout, result or journals for richer text.

The owning observability property `Legacy completion admission cannot recover upstream counts or privacy from tracked excerpt format` uses data-only on-disk snapshots, not new runners/native truth. Two different4/4000-character tails after an over-budget common prefix produce identical members. An authored literal4000-unit body ending in an ellipsis produces the same member as a longer clipped ASCII source, so neither the glyph nor the saved length proves loss or zero loss. A valid CJK source hits the8192byte limit mid-codepoint and becomes2730CJK characters plus U+FFFD, below4000units and without an ellipsis; the original bytes/counts are not repaired or inferred. A caller-authored fixture-private prefix remains in legacy text, demonstrating the tracked flag is not its privacy projection. Actual source/npm replay verifies frozen member/render bytes through queued/presented phases after deleting the observed stdout fixture; no refresh or privacy certificate is created.

The first AX contract now has one creation path. All new completion admission captures ax_records without an excerpt_format or presentation selector, including direct helper and ordinary UI callers after unchanged owner/location checks. Existing batches are resolved before any new capture. Internal numbered markers and absent/tracked/navigation aspects remain only for exact retained-snapshot replay, not public product versions or alternate new-message defaults. Journals do not retain complete Batch content, so their private codecs cannot be deleted or relabeled without breaking delivery equality. Steer envelopes already store content and need no alternate formatter. See [retained capture compatibility](coordinator-delivery.md#retained-capture-compatibility). This changes neither source selection nor forward-readability guarantees for older binaries.

Retained result_layout:headings captures preserve the earlier contract: every shown item starts with #0:, #1: and so on, even an only item at #0. Fields stay at root depth; successive blocks have one blank separator line. There is no result suffix or extra TUI heading. Each block has the same per-result fields, without members, a Batch caption, outer routine counters, time windows, repeated runtime summaries or an unconditional full-map hint in model content. The minimum closed terminal delivery marker preserves details-stripped correlation without turning authored labels into metadata or acknowledging identical bodies across epochs. Required source/generation/technical/location facts stay with their result. Only real omission/inventory and recorded non-done omitted states remain outside the list. Valid compact ranges can hide whole compiler JSON reference/detail blocks and the marker, never authored text or warnings. The own-data layout aspect is captured once and is not a public AX selector/version; older indexed, single_result:true and result_layout:records/run_headers canonical captures and numbered codecs keep exact bytes/hints and phase recovery. Inherited/accessor/mixed aspects cannot restyle saved data. A group with one visible row remains indexed, and whole-result omission stays explicit. Both shapes use the same record owner and a compact two-column native TUI over the same full model content; this preserves embedded Markdown/backticks/CRLF as opaque values, keeps selective file investigation explicit rather than adding a standing instruction, and renders tracked previews/source references literally. It emits [omitted: N chars] only from the admitted primary/diagnostic counters, never raw legacy tails. Ellipses sit outside literal previews at admitted head/tail byte gaps; range/hash/source/preview data stays identical. A complete admitted tracked excerpt has no synthetic zero-omission decoration. Summary display keeps the existing200UTF-16 allowance with pair-aligned prefix clipping instead of whitespace/backtick normalization; local loss is [omitted: unknown]. A separate visible legacy basis/upstream marker means original completeness/privacy is unproven, not that a particular hidden tail or positive loss was discovered. Recorded output is neither refreshed nor promoted to a complete primary source.

Whole outcome rows are admitted before lower-priority location references under their retained16KiB/64member limits (fresh shared-result captures now use48KiB); new references render inside their matching record and wholly omitted rows remain covered by omission/inventory. Exact refs fit whole or the entire member/reference row is omitted, without shortened paths or an extra budget. Overflow uses the existing content-bound metadata-only member inventory, with unchanged exclusions, hash and first-write/phase effects. #### Publication Admission

Permission follows a legitimate call through that kernel-owned port under its fixed source policy, not merely a zero exit code. Admission must establish all of the following:

- The selected result belongs to this invocation's observed command and attempt, with stdout as the selected stream.
- The executor chose that result as the ordinary primary response, rather than an arbitrary recorded capture or neighboring result.
- A kernel-owned publication/privacy policy applies to this source kind; actor values cannot widen it.
- The complete selected text is available before display clipping, and the privacy projection applies to that complete input.
- The original producer, owner and exact Run generation remain valid at publication.

Retain that relationship in the invocation context. Serialized `run_instance_id`, execution references, manifest records, paths, marker acceptance or `visible: true` flags cannot manufacture it. A readable capture proves neither exposure permission nor a complete primary result.

#### Complete Export Before Clipping

Process the selected complete input in this order: fixed privacy projection without display clipping, full retained export, byte/Unicode/range metadata, then bounded preview. The existing Recipe/session privacy machinery may be reused, but its default 4000-character evidence clipping must not silently truncate the full export. Completeness and all descriptor counts refer to the authorized exported text, not an undisclosed private original; disclose redaction as specified below.

The standard 1 MiB memory capture limit is not a workload quota or permission to treat every `result.stdout` as complete. Complete memory input still requires exact full-file agreement. For a truncated memory tail, `command-templates.ts` now hashes the actually observed stdout buffers before clipping and supplies optional kernel-only `stdoutSha256` through the existing frozen settled-attempt receipt only when canonical capture persistence completed. The runner already retains that exact callback receipt in its invocation-local primary context; no new registry, producer reconstruction or actor-facing result field is introduced. The raw digest is not serialized into execution manifests or canonical actor results, and it is not UTF-8/privacy/native/publication authority by itself.

Tail publication additionally requires exactly one successful selected attempt, complete persistence, safe observed bytes greater than memory bytes, matching saved/physical length, the exact memory suffix and SHA-256 agreement for the entire checked source. File length and tail equality alone cannot bind an unseen prefix. Decode the full bounded capture with strict UTF-8, then apply the existing captured privacy projection before export/counts/preview. The digest binds raw observed bytes, whereas the exported source digest/counts describe the privacy-projected bytes. Missing/malformed callback digest, partial storage, invalid text or source-input overflow stays explicit unavailability; contradictory source binding remains an authority refusal, not a synthetic workload failure. Existing 4 MiB source-input allowance, descriptor/view limits and workload capture behavior remain unchanged. Larger work output is not forbidden, but its complete publication remains unavailable rather than silently substituting the tail. Formatted runner stdout, actor/result digest flags, session previews and generic temp-output paths cannot substitute for the admitted original.

The port returns either a curated generation-bound source descriptor or explicit source unavailability with a reason. The initial private descriptor also records SHA-256 of the exported UTF-8 bytes; that digest identifies bytes, not privacy permission or native authority. Do not expose raw capture paths, private input, producer references or the port itself. Keep full source bodies separate from bounded metadata views; do not increase compact-view or delivery budgets merely to inline a long source.

#### Publication Ordering and Failure Truth

Prepare text/projection outside any lock spanning awaited work. Revalidate producer, generation and source binding for final synchronous publication under the existing lifecycle admission; never await workload, model or asynchronous preparation callbacks under that lock. A result may promise a source only after that source is successfully committed.

A returned source descriptor is a candidate, not terminal acceptance. The result writer stamps `primary_text_protocol: "run-primary-text-v1"` only through its kernel publication port and strips actor-supplied output/protocol fields. Recovery must admit the saved canonical record and source, not treat that marker by itself as a capability or privacy certificate. At first-result publication, persist the selected descriptor or explicit source-unavailability in the same atomic canonical result record as the corresponding result. Natural acceptance fixes that outcome's binding; `after_stop_claim` publication binds capture evidence only. An existing admitted winning record and its binding remain unchanged, including when the winner records unavailability. A later candidate must not attach its source to that winner. Return values or exceptions alone do not prove acceptance; use canonical result classification, never treating malformed or unproven state as a winner.

A source committed without a corresponding canonical result binding remains unreferenced evidence, not a reconstructed primary outcome. After result commit, recovery reads the saved binding and canonical acceptance classification, then admits the existing source without reconstructing producer/publication capability, scanning for a substitute, republishing or promoting a stop capture to a terminal outcome. An unavailable or changed source stays explicit; never repair the historical binding by silently selecting different bytes.

Give each published source identity fixed exported bytes, including within one generation. An identical repeated publication reuses the committed source identity and full-source metadata; a contradictory publication refuses without overwriting that source. Presentation may derive a smaller preview from those same bytes under the clipping rules below, not mutate the accepted outcome binding. Old references must never resolve to replacement-generation bytes; after retention ends or proof is lost, report unavailable instead. Source, result metadata, index and delivery writes remain separate effects, not one transaction. Preserve actual committed/partial effects and exact errors without implicit retry or rollback.

Classify failures by authority and actual commit stage:

- Export failure: Record explicit `output_unavailable`; do not fabricate a workload exit code or claim complete capture. A live error handler must not turn a failed text export into an invented command failure.
- Admission refusal: Preserve producer/generation/source-binding authority errors; never downgrade them into ordinary source-unavailable metadata or write into replacement state.
- Failure before canonical result commit: Preserve the exact error and any already committed source effects; no natural outcome or accepted binding follows merely from source existence. Do not infer successful rollback or retry implicitly.
- Failure after canonical result commit: Preserve the admitted result and its binding even if Trace, delivery or lifecycle cleanup later fails. Cleanup uncertainty remains uncertainty, not proof of lock release or permission for another producer operation.

Preserve existing natural-result/stop-claim precedence: a source file alone cannot authorize natural terminal acceptance, stop finalization or work resumption.

#### Readonly Saved Source Admission

Private `readRunPrimaryTextSource(stateDir, expected, query?)` now reads a saved publication without the original producer, including after its process exits. It admits the published owned Run, exact generation/optional owner and cooperating outcome protocol, then uses one bounded strict canonical result snapshot for both classification and binding. The shared `runs-outcomes.ts` decoder curates only data; a decoded descriptor, protocol marker, cached index, path or digest alone grants neither body nor producer authority. An orphan export, legacy/unknown publication, malformed/foreign result or changed binding cannot substitute for that admission. Saved explicit unavailability stays explicit, and stop captures remain `capture_only`, never terminal proof; accepted natural sources do not depend on later stop records.

The reader follows only the generation/owner-derived primary filename fixed by that canonical binding. It checks a regular non-symlink evidence directory, bounded strict-UTF8 source descriptor, full SHA-256/byte/code-point agreement and the exact recorded preview/range. Root, marker, metadata, result, directory and source observations are fenced; observed change or failed read/close yields unavailability, not a partial success or implicit retry. Source input remains capped at 4 MiB and canonical JSON inputs at 1 MiB; these are observation allowances, not workload quotas. Available nofollow/nonblocking flags and regular opened-descriptor checks also protect bounded strict ownership/identity metadata reads. Legacy best-effort JSON defaults are unchanged. This does not establish exhaustive filesystem-race immunity, body authenticity, durable immutability or native scope closure.

Without a query, the response preserves the recorded descriptor. An explicit `{ start, end }` selects a half-open UTF-8 byte range; both ends must be scalar boundaries, even for an empty range. It derives the exact excerpt and shown/total/omitted code-point counts without changing the canonical binding, preserves a real BOM/U+FFFD and refuses an encoded response over 64 KiB instead of silently clipping. Its returned `unchanged()` callback is a fresh mutable-file observation, not a capability or archive certificate. The reader neither reapplies live metadata privacy nor opens raw captures/sessions, discovers substitute sources, reconstructs producers, acquires lifecycle/work admission, writes sources/indexes/results, probes processes nor acknowledges delivery. Controller/public routing and linked public-launch acceptance remain separate integrations.

#### Delivery Obligation and Acceptance Boundary

Completion delivery carries the admitted descriptor rather than reconstructing it from root stdout. Operator and model projections must retain the corresponding preview, visible clipping notice, counts and source/generation reference. Renderers must not silently rewrite the excerpt to which the descriptor's ranges and counts apply. Source reads neither refresh publication nor acknowledge or repeat delivery.

Completion members now carry an optional `primary_text` binding captured through the admitted readonly source port, not by copying raw `result.output` or promoting its protocol marker to body permission. Owner/generation follow the exact transition, and only an accepted natural source may become a primary excerpt. Ambient terminal observation and the shared ready-forest candidate check do not read full primary bodies; eligible member collection performs the bounded source verification once before packet admission. A pending existing packet is reused without source reobservation.

Packet admission curates and copies the binding, freezes its nested source/range plus member/artifact records and member arrays, then persists that immutable membership independently of mutable delivery phases. Serialization/reload and pending/queued/presented transitions retain the same descriptor or unavailability. Rendering performs no source/index/result I/O, availability refresh or producer reconstruction. A later missing source does not change an already captured packet; the reference is labeled retained **at capture**, not promised perpetually readable. Missing or changed sources before capture become explicit unavailability without substituting formatted runner stdout. Existing unsupported session/composition/failure displays remain separately labeled legacy diagnostics, never a complete primary source; this does not extend full-source permission to those cases.

The shared operator/model Markdown projection uses literal dynamically fenced previews without whitespace, CRLF, BOM or backtick normalization. It keeps exact shown/total/omitted code-point counts, the half-open UTF-8 range, redaction/clipping distinction and full source/generation/digest reference. Markup delimiters are presentation, not source code points. Existing 64-row/16-KiB model and 256-member/1-MiB journal bounds remain; overflow omits whole rendered rows with explicit retained-member counts, not untracked edits inside excerpts or source paths. Newly admitted row-overflow packets also retain a [scoped captured-member inventory](coordinator-delivery.md#captured-member-inventory) and budget its complete reference before rows. That source lists all captured identities/statuses/lineage and existing primary bindings only; private locations, diagnostics and artifact inventories are not included. Source/journal publication remain separate effects, and phase transitions/rendering never reread or repair the source. Legacy missing references are explicit. Owning properties and a bounded linked source/packed six-composition byte-overflow witness are verified; broader omitted-available-primary/row-limit journeys and artifact/summary/error inventories remain open. Schema parsing and rendering rely only on admitted packet data; they do not authorize new publication or raw/sibling discovery.

The source and installed-tarball SDK fixture now joins the initial case in one chain: a model-issued public `spawn` launches a real bare Node command, the runner publishes its first canonical binding, the root coordinator supplies one automatic batch to the next model context, and ordinary bounded file reads recover the complete exported 128-KiB memory-input or 2-MiB capture-input source from the **reference parsed from that model context**. This does not complete broader summary/error, all artifact/row-limit/omitted-primary, manual visual/native or integrated release acceptance.

#### Minimal Independent Validation Properties

These are required properties, not a declaration of complete acceptance. Owning suites now exercise complete projected retention, immutable source/result binding, metadata capture, live export/result-commit faults and readonly source admission. Real detached-runner coverage recovers the complete 128 KiB source through bounded ranges after proven producer exit, without its runtime-local capability. Capture properties now verify raw-byte digest agreement across chunk/UTF-8 boundaries, omission after persistence failure, non-exposure of the raw digest, full-prefix privacy before clipping and refusal of same-length/same-tail prefix substitution. Reader properties cover orphan/foreign/sibling refusal, late malformed stop independence, capture-only classification, same-length tail replacement, exact BOM/scalar/count behavior, bounded encoded views and read/close/result-replacement faults. Transport properties cover immutable packet reload/phases, literal rendering and shared forest budgets. The source/tarball SDK journey now uses a real model-issued public spawn and recovers complete authorized bytes from the delivered model reference without status polling or native send queues. Broader selection/overflow and public evidence-role/range journeys remain required. Extend existing suites rather than multiplying equivalent schedules:

- Durable join: Cut after source commit and after result commit, discard invocation-local state, then verify that only the latter restores the accepted binding without publisher/work capability. Test source unavailability once in its reader cohort, not at every crash point.
- First-result binding: Extend `tests/runs-results.test.ts` first-result and post-commit Trace checks with descriptor/unavailability immutability; do not duplicate their existing arbitration coverage.
- Stop precedence: Extend the durable stop-claims group in `tests/runs-control.test.ts` with binding assertions for natural-first and stop-first, including source publication before/after the claim. A late result remains capture evidence, never natural or forced-stop terminal proof.
- Stable source bytes: Check identical repetition, refusal of contradictory bytes and no cross-generation substitution. Different equal-length texts must not pass merely because byte counts match.
- Live failure classification: Inject export I/O failure, admission refusal and failures before/after outcome commit through the owning publication paths. Assert exact errors/committed effects, no invented workload code and no overwritten winner. Reuse the existing post-commit Trace witness; a crash cut cannot replace these live-handler checks.
- Descriptor transport: Check serialization/reload fidelity and independence from later mutation of the caller's descriptor. Reuse `tests/run-delivery.test.ts` and `tests/run-ui-runtime.test.ts` acceptance-state coverage instead of copying the present/absent/unknown matrix.

Use production writers/readers with controlled ordering and faults, not a test-only state machine that restates expected answers. Protocol cut points prove neither native crash/lock behavior nor power-loss durability. The linked public-launch fixture proves its initial bare-command primary cases and bounded six-composition captured-member byte-overflow recovery; UTF-8/privacy/count/rendering fault properties, broader selection, native acceptance and release gates remain distinct obligations.

### Source and Truncation Shape

For an embedded primary text result, use this planned `output` descriptor instead of an ambiguous clipped string:

```json
{
  "source": {
    "path": "<readable retained exported result>",
    "run_instance_id": "<same generation>"
  },
  "bytes_total": 8192,
  "shown_bytes": { "start": 0, "end": 3 },
  "chars_total": 6000,
  "chars_shown": 3,
  "chars_omitted": 5997,
  "preview": "ok\n",
  "truncated": true,
  "redacted": false
}
```

The source must exist and retain the complete exported result before clipping. It must be generation-bound or immutable, regular-file verified, and authorized for exposure. Applicable redaction precedes export: counts, ranges, and preview refer to that exact exported source, not an undisclosed private original. `redacted` discloses that distinction. A retained source is not an invitation to expose raw Control journals, private captures, secrets, or arbitrary repository files.

`bytes_total` is the exported source's nonnegative UTF-8 byte length. `shown_bytes` is one half-open range `[start, end)` aligned to UTF-8 boundaries; `preview` is its exact decoded text. `chars_total`, `chars_shown`, and `chars_omitted` are nonnegative Unicode code-point counts of the complete exported text, the exact preview, and the omitted source text. They are not byte counts, UTF-16 code-unit counts, rendered markup lengths, or grapheme-cluster counts. Require `chars_shown + chars_omitted = chars_total`; `truncated` is true exactly when source text is omitted. Capture or stream these counts without loading an entire large document just to measure it. Do not append an untracked ellipsis or cut a source path to fit. No primary result is represented as `output: null`, not fabricated empty content; capture loss produces explicit `output_unavailable` diagnostics, not a pretend complete source. Sources are only promised within declared retention; later replacement/pruning must report unavailable rather than silently reading different bytes.

Artifact bodies are not automatically all inlined. Export a bounded manifest of names, authorized source references, media types, availability, and byte sizes where known; optional text previews use the same descriptor. Binary files remain references. Reuse existing digests when available rather than requiring an implicit full scan of every large artifact. Any mutable external artifact reference must be identified as mutable and cannot serve as the retained original of an embedded excerpt without an owned stable capture.

The shared packet budget covers metadata and previews together. If artifact entries, summaries/errors, or terminal rows are omitted, disclose counts and a readable generation-bound manifest/result-index source for the omitted evidence. Summary/error clipping must likewise retain a source. Every overflow path must be usable with normal authorized file-reading tools; `inspect` cannot be the only way to recover an automatically omitted result.

### Visible Truncation Contract (UX/AX)

This requirement applies to ordinary automatic completion batches, including a subagent's final answer, report, or document excerpt, not only to periodic diagnostics or manual inspection. A truncated text result must carry an immediately readable notice alongside its preview in both the operator card and model-bound content; structured fields hidden only in details are insufficient.

For the descriptor above, a visible rendering can be:

```text
review — result excerpt
ok
[Truncated: shown 3 of 6000 Unicode characters; omitted 5997.]
Full text: <readable retained exported result>
```

- Bind the notice, counts, full-source reference, and generation to each individual result. A merged batch must not replace them with one vague footer such as "some outputs were truncated" or an ambiguous link shared across unrelated reports.
- Preserve attribution and the same complete-source metadata in human and model projections. Reserve budget for the notice and readable full-text reference before filling the preview. If a first-party projection clips further, recompute shown/omitted counts against the same complete source; never silently remove the notice or cut its path.
- Count from the original exported source, not an already-clipped intermediate string. Across several clipping stages, report the final excerpt's actual shown count and total omitted count once, without adding overlapping omissions or resetting the source total.
- Keep presentation clipping separate from interrupted-task partiality. A full retained report may have a truncated preview; an unfinished task may have a complete capture of its partial report. The notice must not imply either that work failed because display was shortened or that the task finished because its preview is readable.
- An authored/paraphrased summary is not a literal excerpt. Label it as a summary and link its source; do not manufacture an omission count by subtracting the summary's length from the original report. If the summary text itself is clipped, its own complete text is the counted source.
- If legacy or lost capture evidence cannot prove the complete text or its length, state that the full source/total length is unavailable. Do not invent a total, omitted count, complete document, or source path. New exports must preserve the complete authorized source before presentation clipping.
- Reference-only artifacts are not silently clipped previews. Label them as references; binary artifacts keep byte/format metadata. When entire results cannot fit, the batch's explicit omission count and readable result index must retain their per-result source/count metadata instead of dropping recoverability.

## Ownership and Implementation Scope

- Recipe resolution owns literal-field validation, direct-delegation inheritance, normalization, and effective-policy capture. Run creation owns the start anchor and singleton comparison.
- Runtime lifecycle supervision owns due times, exact-generation stop claims, process/tree proof, and terminal truth. Presentation code must not signal processes directly.
- Observation owns the shared manual/timed snapshot and declared result/capture evidence. The export owner publishes the readable context index through existing privacy/ownership projections; `inspect` only reads it. Delivery owns frozen packets, bounds, acceptance, and recovery; context projection owns automatic diagnostic supersession only.
- `index.ts` remains wiring. Reuse canonical locks, process verification, journal/capture authorities, and public Pi ports; keep the local module graph acyclic.
- Implementation must update owning Skills, current API references, source/packed contracts, and error guidance when the new behavior actually exists. Do not present unsupported additions as current behavior or use live stores as validation fixtures.

## Acceptance Scenarios

These are required future witnesses for the complete supervision feature, not tests claimed to exist or pass today. Each assertion, witness and publication blocker below belongs to that candidate's source, tracked dist, installed package and declared conditions. The separately scoped AX/navigation/existing-runtime release in BACKLOG.md does not claim the complete matrix: excluded features stay inactive, while applicable privacy, ownership, generation, replay, navigation and included-behavior obligations remain mandatory. Scoping a release never excuses a safety regression.

Evidence classes: **U** is deterministic unit/property coverage, controlled clocks and injected failures; **I** is integration through the real runtime/public paths; **N** is the real native backend on the explicitly agreed platform set; **P** is the installed package through public SDK/tool paths, not private-helper calls. Controlled providers may model missing LLM responses, but mocks, injected platforms and skipped native cases never establish native containment, controller independence or closure.

Expected legacy/unsupported refusal or `unproven` can pass its negative scenario. It cannot replace a positive supported-backend `max_runtime` witness or silently narrow the agreed platform/credential contract. All-known-member settlement and empty resources with uncontrolled egress remain insufficient. One blocked mandatory assertion or missing applicable proof blocks publication of the supervision feature; a privacy/authority violation in the retained candidate also blocks the selective release.

The [canonical backlog](../BACKLOG.md) keeps A/B/C independently actionable where their inputs permit, with A4 depending on B1 and real enforcement depending on B plus C. D joins the complete future supervision matrix into one candidate; branch completion alone is not release authority. The operator-confirmed selective AX release has its own bounded candidate checkpoint there, without accepting or weakening the deferred supervision feature. Package/audit/authority checks still apply before publication, and exact npm/GitHub identity verification closes release execution afterward; this matrix does not replace the [release contract](./releasing.md).

- **SUP-01 — Policy matrix:** Prove all four combinations, independent Recipe/launch precedence, explicit null disabling, and unchanged no-policy jobs/services. Reject malformed/overflowing values and `report_every < 60000ms` before workload launch; accept equivalent one-minute literals without rounding and keep short positive `max_runtime` values valid. Actor arguments cannot override runtime policy accidentally.
  - Evidence: U boundary/invalid-value properties plus I/P literal-field admission, including explicit null and unchanged actor arguments.
  - Publication blocker: Coercion, invalid lower-layer acceptance, hidden defaults, policy loss or a silently weakened launch.
- **SUP-02 — Scope and reuse:** Prove direct specialization and async registered-tool inheritance, no import/branch timer resets, synchronous-path rejection, equivalent normalized singleton reuse without clock reset, conflicting-policy rejection, and fresh clocks for a new generation.
  - Evidence: U/I/P direct specialization, async-tool/import/sync paths and same-generation singleton attachment/replacement.
  - Publication blocker: Wrong inheritance/scope, incompatible singleton acceptance or a refreshed allowance on attachment/nested work.
- **SUP-03 — Time and recovery:** Count startup/retries/delays in elapsed time; retain anchors across reattachment, act on overdue deadlines without a fresh allowance, disclose lost clock proof, and avoid long-duration timer clamping or missed-tick replay.
  - Evidence: U clock jumps, arithmetic bounds and long-timer scheduling plus I reattachment/restart/recovery.
  - Publication blocker: Invented age/deadline, refreshed budget, missed overdue enforcement or uncertainty presented as known timing.
- **SUP-04 — Independent supervision:** Keep reporting/termination operative with an idle/busy coordinator, a silent actor, and a hung workload. Neither missing Trace nor blocked model delivery may extend the deadline.
  - Evidence: U eligibility plus I/N hung workload/provider and busy/idle controller independence; a controlled provider may model missing LLM responses.
  - Publication blocker: Enforcement waits for model delivery, inspect, a workload callback or routine reporting; no positive supported-native path.
- **SUP-05 — Minimal snapshots:** Check exact generation, elapsed/status, launch cwd versus the readable context-index pointer, explicit index unavailability, bounded shape, and no invented health/progress or leaked unrelated content.
  - Evidence: U/I running, accepted-natural, stopping, legacy, unavailable-index and generation-replacement observations.
  - Publication blocker: Contradictory/unbounded payload, fabricated timing/health/scope proof, unrelated content or read-side state changes.
- **SUP-06 — Latest-only delivery:** Many ticks produce one latest pending snapshot; many Runs produce bounded fair batches and one debounced idle wakeup. Exercise budget pressure without duplicate ticks or silent loss of deferred state.
  - Evidence: U/I/P busy/idle, many-Run fairness, unresolved acceptance and shared-budget pressure.
  - Publication blocker: Polling required for delivery, mutable admitted packets, duplicate/catch-up ticks, starvation, silent loss or synthetic steer.
- **SUP-07 — Context isolation:** Repeated accepted snapshots leave one current diagnostic per generation. Preserve native history, foreign entries, and terminal members of mixed packets; supersession cannot finalize unaccepted packets.
  - Evidence: U/I/P real model-context/session projections containing foreign/native/manual entries and mixed terminal packets.
  - Publication blocker: Foreign/history deletion, lost terminal members or supersession that finalizes an unaccepted packet.
- **SUP-08 — Terminal/restart races:** Completion or replacement suppresses stale queued/in-flight diagnostics; old timers cannot kill a new generation. Prove crash/recovery and session-switch handling without accepting a proposal as delivery.
  - Evidence: U/I/P completion/proposal/acceptance/replacement orderings and crash/session-switch recovery; N verifies native dispatch boundaries.
  - Publication blocker: Old-generation delivery/actuation, stale running after terminal, lost final outcome or proposal treated as acceptance.
- **SUP-09 — Kill intent:** Missing/stale generations, foreign owners, PID reuse, and unavailable process proof fail before signals or Control admission. Exact already-terminal calls are idempotent; saturated actor-local Control cannot block runtime kill.
  - Evidence: U/I/P public generation/owner preconditions plus N replacement/PID-reuse/dispatch witnesses.
  - Publication blocker: Signals or Control before validation, substitution of the newest generation, foreign authority or kill blocked by actor Control saturation.
- **SUP-10 — Stop races and truth:** Natural completion, deadline, and manual kill serialize to one outcome. Distinguish manual kill from timeout; unresolved death stays nonterminal; stop only proven owned descendants and retain partial evidence/accepted effects.
  - Evidence: N sealed native/child admission, membership/egress, producer loss and recursive closure, supported by the mechanism's completeness argument.
  - Publication blocker: Signal/runner/group exit, elapsed wait, uncontrolled-resource emptiness or all-known-member settlement substituted for whole-scope proof; legacy optimistic finalization.
- **SUP-11 — Honest excerpts:** Verify UTF-8 ranges, full/empty/large results, byte and Unicode code-point counts, redaction-relative metadata, missing/incomplete captures, unavailable old generations, and independent task partiality. Never substitute newer bytes or invent source availability.
  - Evidence: U/I/P UTF-8/Unicode/redaction properties, full/empty/large inputs, multistage previews and unavailable generations/captures.
  - Publication blocker: Wrong accounting, newer-byte substitution, an unreadable/unretained promised source or incomplete capture presented as complete.
- **SUP-12 — Recoverable overflow:** Large/binary/many artifacts and omitted terminal rows retain a readable manifest/index with omission counts under the shared context budget. Reading published evidence does not require `inspect` or raw-registry access.
  - Evidence: U/I/P many outcomes/artifacts, large/binary references and bounded packet overflow with selective readable recovery.
  - Publication blocker: Silent omissions, unrecoverable entries, raw-registry dependence or unbounded/binary bodies in model context.
- **SUP-13 — Public delivery journey:** Real source and packed SDK fixtures prove one active-boundary update after the entire tool batch, idle batching, root/child lineage, silent-policy preservation, no periodic wakeup when disabled, no native-steer synthesis, and independent urgent semantic attention. Assertions must witness provider callback completion outside the callback.
  - Evidence: I/P public SDK lifecycle, full-tool-batch active boundaries, idle batching, lineage and attention; fixture registries stay isolated.
  - Publication blocker: Only private-helper success, source/package drift, public-port bypass, live-registry mutation or assertions completed only inside provider callbacks.
- **SUP-14 — Compact inspect parity:** Prove Run default/status equivalence, one shared timed/manual producer, equivalent bounded JSON/text scope, terminal elapsed freezing, rejected inapplicable options, and retained explicit management/expert routes. Manual inspection must not change clocks, delivery eligibility/acceptance, lifecycle state, or export files; ordinary manual tool evidence remains intact.
  - Evidence: U/I/P same-observation manual/timed JSON/text parity, expert routes, invalid-option rejection and no-side-effect checks.
  - Publication blocker: Divergent status truth, raw dumps/scans, manual ACK/cooldown/budget/lifecycle changes or exports created by inspection.
- **SUP-15 — Same-session selective file investigation:** Kernel gives the owning logical Pi session a root-first prioritized map from the existing shared Run-directory index owner, not a second registry. Session → Run → unique real directory/generation entries are latest-registration-first; each Run's role index remains separate. Same saved-session restart/resume after producer exit works with ordinary tools and no original producer/publication keys or old sessionManager. Preserve exact ownership/generation, known coverage/size/format, containment and curated automatic preview privacy; do not promise unsaved generation history or change physical layout.
  - Evidence: U/I/P root-first ordinary reads and actual same-logical-session restart/resume without original keys; independent-process different-session and same-session/different-Run writers; locked fresh merge including maintenance, duplicate/reused directories, registration order independent of read/status/clock touches, atomic readable JSON and corrupt-state preservation; foreign/forked/cloned/supplied-owner/stale/path-substitution rejection; no eager load/read-triggered rebuild/export/repair/work/native/timing effects.
  - Publication blocker: Lost unrelated registrations, unlocked/stale-snapshot writes, cross-session catalog projection, capability loss blocking valid same-session research, wrong-generation/unreadable promised sources, path escape, fictitious retained history/completeness or raw private bodies substituted into automatic curated projections.
- **SUP-16 — Visible ordinary-result clipping:** In both operator and model views, exercise final answers/reports/documents, multiple merged results, Unicode astral/combining sequences, empty/full/zero-preview cases, multistage clipping, and packet overflow. Assert exact per-source shown/total/omitted character counts, adjacent full-text references, preserved generation attribution, notice-first budgeting, summary-versus-excerpt distinction, and honest unknown-length/source failures. Verify actual ordinary completion delivery, not just descriptor construction.
  - Evidence: U/I/P actual ordinary operator/model completions, merged results, Unicode, zero/full previews, multistage clipping and overflow.
  - Publication blocker: Silent ordinary-result clipping, missing notice/reference, reset/double-counted accounting or display truncation confused with task partiality.
- **SUP-17 — Root-session diagnostic cadence:** Stagger many Runs' due times across one top-level session and prove at least `60000ms` between accepted routine batches, with latest-only fair merging rather than per-Run wakeups or catch-up bursts. Exercise the exact boundary, unresolved proposal/acceptance, busy/idle and recovery, independent owner sessions, and manual inspection without changing the gate. Terminal outcomes, short execution deadlines, and explicit urgent `steer` must bypass the cooldown; absent/null reporting must produce no periodic wakeups.
  - Evidence: U/I/P staggered due times, exact floor, unresolved acceptance, busy/idle/recovery, independent roots and bypass neutrality.
  - Publication blocker: Per-Run frequency bypass, cooldown from proposals, manual/terminal/urgent gate changes, catch-up bursts or diagnostic wakeups while disabled.

Use existing Recipe/reference, tool/Control, async-run, observability, delivery, session-evidence, inspect/security, and agent-journey test families. Runtime implementation closes only after focused witnesses plus the normal product validation, source/packed parity, and canonical ABCd context checks. Version preparation and external publication belong to the applicable scoped candidate checkpoint in BACKLOG.md, not implementation progress alone; follow the [release contract](./releasing.md). Removing `inspect` and implementing MPRIS remain outside the authorized scope.
